Research dossier: NVDA — NVIDIA Corp

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.7 · Opportunity score: 70

Next earnings: 2026-08-26

Business

NVIDIA sells the accelerated-compute substrate for AI: data-center GPUs (Blackwell 300 now,

Rubin ramping), the networking that lashes them into clusters (InfiniBand, Spectrum-X, NVLink),

and the CUDA software moat that keeps the ecosystem sticky. Customers are the hyperscalers

(~50% of Data Center revenue), plus a widening tail of AI clouds, sovereigns, enterprise and

industrial. Revenue durability is the whole question (per Nvidia - AI Cycle Durability and GPU Economics):

it rests on (a) hyperscaler capex continuing to ramp, and (b) GPU economics — rental prices and

useful life — staying firm. Both are intact right now.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes. Q1 FY27 (reported 2026-05-20) was a genuine acceleration, not a peak: revenue $81.6B

(+85% YoY, +20% QoQ), Data Center a record $75.2B (+92% YoY), networking $14.8B (+199% YoY),

and — the tell — Q2 guided to $91B, another ~+11% sequential. Management paired it with an

$80B buyback authorization and a 25× dividend raise ($0.01→$0.25): a balance-sheet flex, not a

defense. Critically, China Hopper/H20 shipments are now zero (vs $4.6B a year ago), so the

entire China data-center business is already scrubbed from the run-rate — it is pure optionality

to the upside, not a risk still to be taken.

Yet the stock is ~15% off its highs and -13% over 20 days, sitting just below its 50-DMA.

The slide is macro and cohort-driven — Hormuz/Middle-East risk-off, sticky CPI (~4.2%), the

FOMC dot-plot binary days away, and a cautious Broadcom Q3 guide that dragged the whole semi

cohort — not a company-specific thesis crack. That is the favorable quadrant of

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: a real dip in a name whose thesis just got stronger,

not FOMO into a run. NVDA's own dip-buyer base rate confirms the setup: 32 instances, **66–68%

win** at +21/42/63d, avg +12.2% at +63d (worst-case -45% — size accordingly, don't go all-in).

Valuation + position vs. range

Price $205.19; market cap ~$4.97T; trailing P/E 31.4, forward P/E 20.7; TTM revenue

$253.5B (+71%), net income $159.6B (+108%); 24.2B shares. The standout fact: the single largest

AI beneficiary trades at a **forward multiple (~20.7×) essentially in line with the S&P 500

itself (~20–22×)** — while growing revenue 85% and earnings 100%+. Implied forward EPS ≈ $9.9.

My fair-value read: a clean, cash-generative 80%+ grower earns more than a market multiple. At a

conservative 22–25× forward EPS that is ~$218–$248. At today's $205 the market is paying ~20.7×

— i.e. pricing in deceleration that the Q2 guide does not yet show. It sits ~13% below its 52-wk

high and +46% above the low: mid-range, not extended. **Much is priced in on growth, but the

multiple is not — that gap is the edge here.**

Post-deal / repriced check

Wait-for-deal thesis does not apply cleanly: NVDA is the substrate, the picks-and-shovels

seller collecting the capex — not a small provider waiting for one lab's offtake deal to reprice

it. There is no single deal whose announcement consumed the entry edge. The relevant repricing

question is the multiple, and it has gone the wrong way for bulls (compressed to ~20× forward),

which is what makes this an entry rather than a chase. On the ai-capex-payback-window

J-curve, NVDA is on the right side: it books the cash now; the FCF compression is the

customers' problem. The thesis break would be customers cutting capex (kill-switch #1) — not

yet visible.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: A 100%-grower with a CUDA moat and a fortress balance sheet at a market forward

multiple, on a 15% macro dip, with China as free upside optionality.

  • Bear: $5T mega-cap whose entire thesis is one variable — hyperscaler capex — and the dot

plot, a Broadcom-style guide, or a single capex-pause cycle de-rates the whole cohort hard.

  • First-principles: At 20× forward earnings of a business compounding 80%+ with the world

short watts and wafers through 2027, you are underpaying for the durability, not overpaying

for the growth.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived this run from $205.19 and forward EPS ≈ $9.9:

  • Buy-below: $200 (≈20× forward — buy at or under a market multiple).
  • Fair: $225 (~22.5× forward).
  • Stretch: $250 (~25× forward — the most I'd pay before demanding a fresh catalyst).

Execution shape, given the FOMC dot plot is 3–4 sessions out: stage it. A partial at/just

under $200–205 now, holding dry powder to add into a dot-plot-driven flush toward $185–190. The

-45% worst-case in the base rates is the reason not to commit the full clip at market.

Macro fit

All three ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches are clear (capex +77% YoY and still being raised;

B200 pricing firm with a 3.6M backlog; HY OAS ~2.75%, 2.2× headroom). The regime is extended and

narrow, and the macro stance is "hold cash into the FOMC, only buy real dips or fresh catalysts"

— NVDA satisfies both exceptions (a real 15% dip and a fresh thesis-confirming print). It

is also far less dot-plot-sensitive than the extreme-P/S names (NBIS, IREN) the macro note

flags, because it sits at a market multiple, not a re-rate-dependent one. On the

Opportunity-cost lens vs. the field, NVDA ranks **at the top of the AI-infra cohort right

now**: lowest multiple, highest quality, real dip, buyback support. The one thing capping the

opportunity score off a perfect read: at $5T the upside is bounded (no 3×), so the asymmetry is

smaller than a small-cap with a setup — hence 70, a genuine standout but not a moonshot.

Falsification

  • 30-day: FOMC dot plot erases the 2026 cut path and NVDA breaks decisively below ~$185 on

cohort de-rate — re-rate the entry lower (thesis intact, geometry wrong). A clean hold/dovish

surprise removes the overhang and the partial becomes a full position.

  • 60-day: Hyperscaler Q2 capex guides decelerate (late-July prints) — the actual

kill-switch #1 trigger and the only thing that breaks the thesis rather than the multiple.

  • 90-day: NVDA Q2 FY27 print (~2026-08-26) shows Data Center revenue decelerating

sequentially or gross margin rolling; OR B200 pricing/lead-times compress toward weeks

(kill-switch #2); OR a Google TPU-v8 / ASIC inflection visibly takes inference share. Any one

flips BUY toward HOLD.

P/E 20.7, TTM rev $253.5B, NI $159.6B, 24.2B shares (fetched — load-bearing valuation row).

  • CNBC / intellectia / indmoney / stocktitan — Q1 FY27 numbers: $81.6B rev (+85%), DC $75.2B

(+92%), compute $60.4B, networking $14.8B (+199%), Q2 guide $91B, $80B buyback, $0.25 dividend,

China Hopper zero vs $4.6B (snippet — corroborated across four outlets; underlying is the

SEC 8-K q1fy27pr.htm).

  • gurufocus / tradingkey / heygotrade — June slide drivers: Hormuz/risk-off, sticky CPI,

Broadcom cautious Q3 guide, AI-bubble chatter; -15% from highs (snippet).

  • bingx / ainvest / tickeron — analyst consensus Strong Buy, avg PT ~$268–276 (range

$205–400); Rubin 6-chip platform, ~10× inference-cost reduction (snippet).

  • research/patterns.py --ticker NVDA — dip-buyer base rate 66–68% win, avg +12.2% at +63d,

worst -45% (local backtest, 32 instances).

METABUYfetched64›

Research dossier: META — Meta Platforms, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 64

Next earnings: 2026-07-29

Business

Meta runs the largest advertising network on earth — Facebook, Instagram, WhatsApp, Messenger

— reaching 3.56B daily active people (Mar 2026, +4% YoY). Advertisers pay for attention; the

revenue durably continues because the user base is global and sticky, the ad ranking/targeting

stack is an AI data moat that compounds, and switching costs for advertisers are low only in

theory (reach and ROAS keep them). Reality Labs is a cash-burning option, not the thesis. The

new layer is Meta as an AI-infra spender: it is building its own hyperscale compute (incl. the

MTIA in-house chip roadmap) to serve recommendations and GenAI — which is what drove the

selloff.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, with disciplined staging. META is the rare AI-infra mega-cap that actually dipped: down

~28.8% from its $796.25 ATH to $566.92, below its 50DMA (−8.8%) and 200DMA (−13.9%), 20-day

return −8.3%. The whole field this lens looks at is extended post-deal names; META is the

exception that is below fair value with a real selloff. Critically, the selloff driver — Q1

capex guidance raised to $125–145B (~2x the $72B 2025 spend) and Q1 capex (~$20B) now exceeding

quarterly FCF (~$12.4B) — is the expected J-curve shape (ai-capex-payback-window), not a

thesis break. Per ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches, the break condition is capex deceleration;

Meta is accelerating, which confirms demand. The market is punishing the exact behavior the

framework reads as bullish. That gap is the opportunity. The honest counterweight is the June 5

equity-raise speculation (a genuine new dilution overhang, not J-curve noise) and a coin-flip

near-term base rate — so this is an accumulate-on-a-trigger, not a back-up-the-truck.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $566.92; market cap $1.44T; 2.54B shares. TTM revenue $214.96B (+26%), net income

$70.59B, EPS $27.49. Trailing P/E 20.63, forward P/E 17.27.

  • A 26–33% revenue grower with 41% operating margins trading at a forward P/E below the S&P 500

(~20–22x forward) is the cheapest mega-cap hyperscaler on the board. My fair-value read: a fair

multiple for this growth/margin profile is ~22–24x forward EPS (~$30) → ~$660–720. Sell-side

consensus PT ~$828 (treat as aggressive). At $567 the stock is meaningfully below my fair

range — the dilution overhang and J-curve FCF compression are doing the discounting.

  • Range position: 9.0% above the 52w low, 28.8% below the high. This is the lower-third of the

range, i.e. a dip-buyer zone, not an extended chase.

Post-deal / repriced check

N/A in the Wait-for-deal thesis sense — META is not a small/mid pre-deal infra name awaiting

a lab partnership; the entry edge here is not a deal catalyst. The relevant repricing is the

downward one: the multiple has DE-rated (forward P/E from a richer level down to 17x) on capex

+ dilution fear. So the old bull case isn't "consumed by a deal that already ran" — if anything

the opposite: the price has come to the thesis. The new fact in the last 7–14 days is the

equity-raise rumor (June 5), which is a fresh negative catalyst, not a confirming one.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The only mega-cap hyperscaler at a sub-market forward multiple, growing 30%, dipped

28% on spending the market will thank it for in 2028 — buy the J-curve, not the FCF headline.

  • Bear: A multi-tens-of-billions equity raise at a depressed price would be value-destructive

dilution (C14), buybacks are already suspended, and the FOMC dot plot (June 16–17) could

flush the whole AI cohort further before any bottom is in.

  • First-principles: $1.44T for $215B of revenue at 41% op margins and a structural ad

monopoly, paying ~17x forward — at this price you are underwriting the ad business alone and

getting the AI buildout as a free (if expensive) option. That math makes sense from scratch.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived from this run's data (forward P/E 17.27, fair ~$660–720, 52w low ~$520, base rates below):

  • Buy-below: $575 — accumulate-on-trigger zone (current price qualifies); enter in

tranches, not at-market.

  • Strong-add: $520–540 — a deeper flush toward the 52w low; the dip-buyer base rate

improves the lower the entry.

  • Fair: ~$690.
  • Stretch (do-not-pay-above without a fresh catalyst): $640.
  • Base-rate discipline: META's own history in the dip_buyer preset (23 instances) wins only

47.8% at +21d (avg −0.7%), improving to 60% at +42d (+3.4%) and 57.9% at +63d (+4.8%), with avg

max drawdown −14.1% (worst −42.5%). Read: near-term is a coin flip with real downside — entry

must be trigger-gated and staged, holding horizon ≥42–63d, not a single at-market buy.

Macro fit

  • Kill-switches: all three clear (capex +77% YoY and rising, GPU demand outrunning supply,

HY OAS ~275bp). Meta's own capex raise is a kill-switch-#1 confirmation, not a warning.

  • Regime: market extended (forward P/E 20–22x), breadth narrow — but META is the dip within

the extended tape, and at forward P/E 17 it is the least exposed mega-cap to the discount-rate

sizing constraint that targets extreme-P/S names (NBIS, IREN). The June 16–17 FOMC dot plot

(first under Warsh, ~98.3% hold) is a live binary 3–4 sessions out; a hawkish "no cuts until

2027" could pressure the cohort. This argues for staging into and through the FOMC, not

front-running it — first tranche small, add on post-FOMC confirmation or a flush to the

strong-add zone.

  • Opportunity-cost rank (Opportunity-cost lens): high. In a field of extended post-deal

names re-bought at today's price, META is one of the few genuinely below fair value with a real

dip and an intact secular thesis — a strong use of the marginal $100, gated on entry discipline.

Falsification

  • 30-day: Meta confirms a large equity (not debt) raise at depressed prices → dilution

thesis-damaging, cut conviction; OR a hawkish FOMC dot plot (June 17) de-rates the cohort →

wait for the strong-add zone. A clean denial / debt-only funding path → overhang lifts, raise

conviction.

  • 60-day: Q2 print (July 29) — watch ad-revenue growth holding ~30% and Q3 capex commentary.

Capex guidance cut toward <10% YoY would trip kill-switch #1 and break the thesis (unlikely);

ad-growth deceleration below ~20% would crack the cash-engine that funds the buildout.

  • 90-day: Any sign AI revenue is ramping slower than the depreciation schedule (the J-curve

falsifier), or GPU/asset useful-life compression — would move the payback point out and justify

the de-rate.

  • CNBC / Fortune / Yahoo / CoinDCX — Q1 2026 print (rev $56.31B +33%, EPS beat, 41% op margin,

AMZNBUYfetched63›

Research dossier: AMZN — Amazon.com, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-10 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 63

Business

Two businesses bolted together. Retail/logistics (~83% of revenue, ~$743B TTM, +14.2%) is the

funding mechanism — 6B packages/yr, 1M+ warehouse robots, the Prime/ads flywheel. AWS (~18% of

revenue, the majority of operating income at ~38% segment margin) is the compounding engine: a

vertically-integrated AI-compute utility that owns land, power contracts, buildings, custom

silicon (Trainium/Graviton/Nitro), the compiler stack, and frontier-lab workloads (Anthropic).

Revenue durably continues because the $364B AWS backlog (ex-Anthropic's $100B) is contracted

multi-year demand on chips Amazon designs itself, with switching costs denominated in petabytes

of egress. This is the cleanest Western hyperscaler expression of the picks-and-shovels thesis

(compute → power → land), and the lowest-beta one.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

For the first time in this name's recent history under our lens, yes — measured. The user's

2026-05-30 dive landed HOLD at $270.64 explicitly because the entry was wrong (near ATH, no

active preset), not because the asset was. It mapped a ladder: $258 Alert, $230 GTC where the

"Dip Buyer" preset activates. Since then AMZN has fallen ~12% to $238.00 — through the $258

rung and into the Dip Buyer zone (14.6% off the $278.56 ATH, sitting on its 200 DMA, −6.1% vs

the 50 DMA). The patience trade the prior run was waiting for has substantially arrived. The

drop is cohort-wide capex anxiety (the $200B 2026 capex guide spooking the whole hyperscaler

complex) + EU cloud-regulation headlines + a May 7–8 AWS US-East-1 outage — macro/cohort and

sentiment, not a thesis break. Per macro-cohort-confirmation, that is exactly the kind of

dip the method says to buy: confirm it's cohort-wide (it is — capex scrutiny hit MSFT/GOOGL/META

too), then act.

Valuation + position vs. range

My own read, re-derived from this run's numbers (rev/share $742.78B ÷ 10.76B sh ≈ $69.03):

  • P/S (working anchor for AMZN — fwd EPS is capex-suppressed): at $238 the stock trades

~3.45× sales, below the 3.9× base band ($269) and well above the 2.7× bear ($186). On the

May read it sat at the $269 base; today it's ~12% under it. Cheaper than fair on the metric

that best fits a name whose earnings are temporarily compressed by the build.

  • Forward P/E: ~24× on the ~$9.86 consensus fwd EPS the user cited (stockanalysis' headline

29× uses a lower, more conservative forward number, and the trailing 29× is inflated by the

~$16B unrealized Anthropic fair-value mark — discount headline EPS for a few quarters).

  • vs. range / analysts: 14.6% off ATH; analyst avg PT $312.79 (median $319.50, low $207,

high $370), strong-buy on 66 analysts → ~+27% to the average. The asymmetry (+31% to avg,

−13% to the $207 bear floor) is finally tilted right, where it wasn't at $270.

What's already priced: the big deals (Anthropic $100B, OpenAI/Trainium, Meta + Snowflake $6B

Graviton). There is no fresh catalyst here — this is a dip-buy on a confirmed thesis, not a

repricing on news.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: AMZN is post-deal and the deal-edge is consumed — but that thesis

governs pre-deal entry timing, and AMZN was never being bought for an un-priced deal. The

relevant discipline here is extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: "extended, don't chase" was

the right call at $270; it no longer applies because the name is no longer extended — it has

corrected into a dip. Buying a confirmed-thesis hyperscaler ~15% off its high, below its P/S base

band, on its 200 DMA, is the opposite of the IREN-at-$61 mistake (which was buying a post-deal

name ~30% above entry with no catalyst). This buy is below the prior entry rungs.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Backlog ($364B, +50% QoQ) and Trainium (>$225B committed, capturing NVDA's margin in-house) mean the $9.86 fwd EPS is the stale number — on true forward earnings this is ~22× for a 28%-growing OI engine, and you're now buying it 12% under its P/S base.
  • Bear: The reaccel is bought, not earned — AWS margin compressed ~230bps YoY, Q2 guide telegraphs OI growth halving to +15%, FCF goes negative in 2026 against a ~$200B capex pace and D/E 53, and the depreciation freight train arrives in 2027–28 right as backlog must convert at slipping margins; pay a full multiple for peak margin into the cliff.
  • First-principles: The market prices a retailer with a cloud arm; the cash flows are an AI utility with a retail funding arm — 3.45× sales for the only electron-to-inference-token integrated hyperscaler is fair-to-cheap; the asset was never the question, the entry was, and the entry just improved.

Entry levels the agent derives

(this run's data; not inherited)

  • Buy-below $240 (starter, where we are): Dip Buyer preset active (~15% off ATH), below P/S

base $269, on the 200 DMA. Small starter size — the macro overlay says scale in, not lump.

  • Fair $269: P/S base (3.9× × $69.03). Analyst avg $312 is the bull-side fair.
  • High-conviction add $230: 200 DMA + Jan swing-low cluster + below the fwd-P/E bull band;

the core of the Dip Buyer zone where this name's base rates are best. This is the real buy.

  • Stretch / max-conviction $210–215: a cohort wash-out into the fwd-P/E base region — back up

the truck only if the dip overshoots on macro, not on an AMZN-specific margin miss.

Cohort-confirm gate (per macro-cohort-confirmation): satisfied — the selloff is capex-anxiety

across the hyperscaler cohort, not name-specific bad news.

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear (ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches): capex +77% YoY (far from the

<10% break — AMZN's own $200B guide is up from $125B, the opposite of deceleration); GPU

pricing/lead-times still supply-constrained; credit BBB ~100bp / HY ~285bp, no stress. Secular

thesis intact → this single-name drawdown is opportunity, not signal. Regime: market extended,

breadth narrow, May CPI headline hot (energy) but core benign, Fed on hold (first cut Q4'26 at

earliest) — a sizing constraint on extreme-P/S names, but AMZN at 3.45× sales isn't that name.

Opportunity-cost rank (Opportunity-cost lens): in a field where most AI-infra names are

extended and post-deal, AMZN is one of the few in a *genuine, cohort-confirmed dip below fair

value*, and it's the lowest-beta diversification leg that doesn't gap 20% on a single capex

quarter. That makes it a relatively strong use of the marginal $100 right now — hence a 63, above

the 20–50 median but short of a 70+ standout because there is no fresh catalyst and the Q2 print

(July 29) is a live margin-compression risk.

Entry-shape base rates (patterns.py, this name's own history): **dip_buyer, 18 instances —

+42d 72% win / +5.2% avg, +63d 72% win / +5.2% avg; avg max drawdown −11.4% (worst −35.1%).**

A constructive setup that the verdict leans on — but the −11% typical drawdown is why this is a

scaled, trigger-gated BUY (starter at $240, weight at $230), not an all-in. extended_run base

rate is poor (43% win, negative avg) and is correctly not the active preset today.

Falsification

  • 30-day: Q2 2026 print (~July 29) — AWS growth decelerating below ~26–27%, or a sharp Q3 OI

guide cut, confirms the bear's margin-compression case → flip to HOLD / pause adds.

  • 60-day: Kill-switch #1 — any hyperscaler cutting 2026/27 capex guidance (toward <10% YoY)

→ thesis break, not a dip. Also: EU cloud regulation materially restricting AWS gov contracts.

  • 90-day: Credit spreads BBB >250bp / HY >600bp, or a Fed "no cuts until 2027" lurch that

de-rates high-multiple compounders broadly; a flush below $196 (52-wk low) on AMZN-specific bad

news (not cohort) would say the dip was earned, not macro.

Business

Bloom Energy makes solid-oxide fuel-cell "Energy Servers" — modular, on-site power that runs on

DGXXBUYmissing62›

Research dossier: DGXX — Digi Power X Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-09 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 62

Business

Digi Power X is an energy-infrastructure company actively pivoting from cryptocurrency mining to AI data center colocation and GPU-as-a-Service. Revenue today comes from three segments — crypto mining (legacy, winding down), energy/electricity sales, and colocation services — but the forward business is almost entirely the new AI infrastructure stack:

1. AI colocation: Purpose-built data center in Columbiana, Alabama for Cerebras Systems under a 10-year, $1.1B MSA (signed May 5, 2026). 40 MW in two phases; Phase 1 (15 MW) targeted ready December 15, 2026; full 40 MW by end of Q1 2027. Total potential value $2.5B with renewals + $1.4B expansion option.

2. NeoCloudz GPU-as-a-Service: Initial AI revenues live; $35M purchase commitment for NVIDIA Vera Rubin (next-gen GPU) to scale the platform.

3. Energy sales: Residual legacy stream; declining.

Why might the Cerebras revenue durably continue? Cerebras is a well-capitalized AI chip company (wafer-scale engines) with real compute demand; the 10-year term with renewal options signals serious intent, not a trial contract. That said, single-customer concentration is a material structural risk — DGXX's entire AI colocation book rides on Cerebras not canceling or renegotiating.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The thesis is: the deal landed, the stock initially overshot to $9.20, pulled back ~33% to $6.21, and forward valuation is now cheap on 2027 guidance. At $6.21, market cap is ~$575M. The $1.1B deal alone represents ~1.9× the current market cap in committed 10-year revenue. Management guides $250–300M in 2027 revenue; at $6.21 that's ~2.1× forward sales — materially cheap vs. AI infra comps (CRWV, APLD, NBIS have all re-rated to 5–15× forward revenue post-deal).

The deal is the thesis-confirming catalyst per Wait-for-deal thesis, and the stock is consolidating ~33% below the blowoff top. The fundamental picture changed on May 5; the stock's current price reflects a partial digestion of that change, not a full repricing to comp multiples. There is a real buy case here.

But this is a pre-delivery story. Phase 1 is not in service yet. The company has $57.81M cash and no long-term debt — not enough to fund the full $45M+ capex already deployed and the remaining buildout of a 40 MW campus. A capital raise (and dilution) is nearly certain before Phase 1 goes live.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price (June 9, 2026): $6.21
  • 52-week range: $1.42 – $9.20
  • Position in range: 67th percentile; off the $9.20 blowoff by ~33%; well above the $1.42 pre-deal low
  • Market cap: ~$575M (90.42M shares)
  • Revenue TTM: ~$37M (legacy mix; legacy is declining; use forward)
  • 2027 revenue guide: $250–300M → midpoint $275M
  • Forward P/S (2027): ~2.1× — cheap for AI infra at this stage
  • Profitability: Pre-profit; Q1 2026 net loss $4.7M on $6.8M revenue; Adj. EBITDA $1.1M (marginally positive, mostly from legacy energy)
  • Cash: $57.81M; no long-term debt — but insufficient to fund the full buildout alone
  • EPS TTM: -$0.66

Fair-value read: If DGXX delivers $275M in 2027 revenue and trades at 3× sales (conservative for a hyperscaler-contracted AI colocation name), fair value is ~$9.10/share. That's ~46% above today's price. At 4× (more in line with peers), it's ~$12.20. The bear case — execution fails or Cerebras renegotiates — sees this back below $3 (legacy-only value).

Post-deal / repriced check

The Cerebras $1.1B deal is the wait-for-deal catalyst. It fired May 5, 2026. The original pre-deal entry edge is consumed. The relevant question now is post-deal: does the post-deal multiple justify ownership?

The stock followed the sustained repricing pattern: ran from ~$3 to $9.20 over two weeks, then gave back ~33% to consolidate around $6–6.50. That $9.20 high looks like deal euphoria — the $6.21 current price reflects a more grounded read on execution risk. At 2.1× 2027 revenue guidance (with a real $1.1B anchor contract), this is not an "entry edge fully consumed" situation; the comps price this kind of deal at a meaningfully higher multiple once operational.

The old bull case ("buy pre-deal") is gone. The new bull case is "buy post-pullback, pre-delivery, at a valuation discount to operational comps." That is a different and valid entry thesis.

Council

  • Bull: $1.1B 10-year contract from a credible AI chip company anchors the entire capital structure; at 2.1× 2027 guidance this is half the multiple of comparable post-deal AI infra names; the Vera Rubin GPU commitment signals NeoCloudz is real.
  • Bear: Single customer (Cerebras) equals single point of failure; capital raise dilution before Phase 1 is nearly certain; the company has never built and operated a data center at this scale; execution miss on the Dec 15 Phase 1 deadline would be a catastrophic re-rating event.
  • First-principles: A company with a $1.1B signed contract, $57.81M cash, no long-term debt, and a clear 18-month revenue ramp is worth more than $575M if it executes — that math works. The discount is the execution and dilution premium, not a fundamental overvaluation.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived from the $6.21 price, 2027 revenue guide ($275M midpoint), comp multiples, and the execution risk discount:

  • Buy below: $6.50 — current price ($6.21) is inside the buy zone; this level gives ~5% buffer vs. today and accounts for the deal reality at a discount-to-comps multiple.
  • Fair value: $9.00 — roughly 3.3× 2027 guide; assumes Phase 1 delivers on time and NeoCloudz ramps to material revenue; aligns with where comps with similar deal quality trade post-delivery.
  • Stretch: $13.00 — if expansion options are exercised ($1.4B additional capacity) and NeoCloudz GPU revenues hit guidance; would be ~4.7× a fully ramped 2027 book.
  • Invalidation: Below $3.50 would suggest the market is pricing execution failure or Cerebras counterparty risk into the stock — a level to reassess the thesis entirely.

Macro fit

Kill-switch status: All three clear as of June 9, 2026. Hyperscaler capex ~$725–750B FY2026 (+67–77% YoY), B200 backlog 3.6M units at multi-month lead times, credit spreads at historically tight levels (BBB ~100bp, HY ~274bp). The AI buildout thesis is intact; DGXX's Cerebras contract is downstream of that buildout.

Regime: Extended market (S&P forward P/E 21.1×, CAPE 42.3×, Buffett 237%), inflation re-accelerating to 3.8%, rate cuts pushed to fall 2026 / Q1 2027. This is a sizing-constraint environment — not a kill-signal, but not a lever-up moment either.

Opportunity-cost rank: DGXX is a post-deal, pre-delivery small-cap in a field where most AI infra names have already delivered and fully repriced. Its edge vs. the field is the valuation gap: 2.1× forward revenue for a company with a $1.1B anchor contract is genuinely cheaper than operational comps. However, IREN, CRWV, APLD are all operational; DGXX is 6 months from Phase 1 delivery. The execution optionality is real but unearned. Score in the 60s reflects: "real setup, not max allocation material."

Sizing note: Small-cap, pre-profitability, single-customer, pre-capital raise = small-position sizing is appropriate. This is not a compounder sized for conviction; it's a catalyst trade sized for the risk profile.

Falsification

30 days (by July 9, 2026):

  • Capex update / construction progress at Columbiana facility — any delay signal is a red flag
  • Capital raise announcement — watch dilution structure; equity raise >15% of shares outstanding would compress the per-share case materially
  • Cerebras news (funding, customer losses, or strategy shift) — any sign of Cerebras financial stress would reprice DGXX immediately

60 days (by August 9, 2026):

  • Phase 1 construction milestone confirmation — on-track or slipping
  • Q2 2026 earnings — NeoCloudz GPU revenue should start appearing; AI colocation revenue begins only post-Phase 1 delivery, so Q2 is still legacy-heavy; watch EBITDA trajectory and cash burn rate

90 days (by September 9, 2026):

  • Phase 1 December 15 delivery trajectory — at this point, management guidance updates (or silence) become the dominant signal
  • FOMC September decision — if rate cuts materialize, multiple-compression risk on high-P/S names eases; if FOMC holds again, this sizing constraint persists
  • Any Cerebras expansion announcement (Phase 2 pull-forward, NeoCloudz partnership) would be thesis-confirming

What flips the verdict to AVOID:

  • Cerebras terminates or materially renegotiates the MSA
  • Phase 1 slips beyond Q1 2027 (execution credibility gone)
  • Dilutive capital raise that swamps the forward revenue per share case
  • Cerebras announces financial distress (funding gap, strategic pivot away from data center compute)

SNPSBUYfetched62›

Research dossier: SNPS — Synopsys, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 62

Next earnings: 2026-08-26

Business

Synopsys is one half of the EDA (electronic design automation) duopoly with Cadence — the

software every semiconductor on Earth is designed and verified on, plus a large silicon-IP

library (interface, foundation, security IP) that chipmakers license rather than build. The

July-2025 Ansys acquisition bolted on multi-physics simulation (structural/thermal/fluids/

electromagnetics), extending the moat from "design the chip" toward "simulate the whole system."

Customers are every fabless designer, IDM, foundry, and increasingly hyperscalers building

custom AI silicon. The revenue is durable because EDA is mission-critical, deeply embedded in

customer workflows, multi-year-licensed, and switching costs are enormous — you do not re-tool a

$100M tape-out mid-flight. This is the **deepest picks-and-shovels layer of the entire AI

buildout**: every Trainium, MI-series, Blackwell-successor, and custom ASIC runs through SNPS/CDNS

tools before it exists in silicon. Structural moat, not a temporal wait-for-deal scarcity play.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, conditionally. The setup: a structural-moat software franchise that has already taken a

large drawdown (−30% off the 52w high of $651.73, −11% over the last 20 trading days) and

re-rated to the low end of its historical multiple (~28–31× forward vs. an EDA-historic

30–45× range), then printed a beat-and-raise on May 27, 2026 — and still sold off ~9% the

next day on near-term China/IP guidance. That is a quality name on a genuine de-rate, with a mild

fresh positive catalyst (raised full-year guide) being ignored because of a known, partially

priced-in overhang. The dip-buyer base rate confirms the shape but warns on timing: at today's

positioning SNPS wins only 32.4% at +21d (avg −1.9%) but 58.3% at +63d (avg +4.9%, n=36), max

drawdown avg −11.2%/worst −27.5%. Read: the edge is on the 2–3-month horizon, not the next few

weeks — entry must be staged/trigger-gated, not all-in at-market, especially with the FOMC

dot plot 3 sessions out.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $453.89 (Jun 12, 2026); 52w range $376.18–$651.73 — trades ~30% below the high, ~21%

above the low.

  • Market cap ~$86.9B; shares ~191.5M (up from ~155M pre-Ansys — real dilution, deal was cash+stock).
  • TTM revenue $8.68B (+39.5% yoy, Ansys-inflated); P/S ~10x — high on its face but it's a 39.5%

non-GAAP-operating-margin software company, so 10x P/S ≈ ~30x earnings, not extreme.

  • GAAP is noisy: net income $773M (−64% yoy), trailing P/E 107, GAAP EPS $4.22 — depressed by

Ansys purchase-accounting amortization, integration, and financing costs. The real number is

non-GAAP FY26 EPS guide of $14.72–$14.80 (mid $14.76) → forward P/E ~30.7× on FY26, ~28.6×

mid-teens-grower duopoly with 39%+ margins). At $453 the name is ~6–7% below my fair value:

modestly cheap, not a screaming bargain. The margin of safety is the de-rate + raised guide;

it's offset by the China/integration risk that caused the de-rate.

Post-deal / repriced check

This is not a Wait-for-deal thesis name and should not be judged by that frame — there is

no lab-partnership scarcity premium to consume; the moat is structural (workflow lock-in), not

temporal. The relevant "deal" is Ansys (closed Jul 2025), and it is not fully repriced upward —

the China overhang. So the entry edge here is the opposite of the AI-infra cohort: the multiple

compressed instead of expanded. Note the temporal conflict in sources: a barchart piece

the current feed (May-27-2026 beat, FY EPS raised to $14.76, forward P/E ~28–31×) and is

treated as a stale prior-episode account, not current state (newest-state-wins).

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The single most durable moat in the AI stack — every chip is designed on it — bought

at the low end of its multiple after a beat-and-raise the market punished for the wrong reason.

  • Bear: China export-control overhang on ECAD/IP is unresolved and open-ended, the IP segment

extended market 3 days from a hawkish-risk dot plot.

  • First-principles: ~30× forward earnings for a duopoly software franchise compounding

mid-teens at 39%+ operating margins with a structural AI-design tailwind is a fair-to-good price

for quality — not a deep-value steal, but a rational long-term own at a below-trend multiple.

Entry levels the agent derives

  • Buy-below: ~$430 (≈29× FY26 EPS, ~5% under spot) — margin of safety + FOMC cushion; a

starter is defensible at spot, full first tranche on a fill here.

  • Fair: ~$485 (≈33× FY26 EPS).
  • Stretch (don't chase above): ~$540 (≈36×).

At $453 the name sits between buy-below and fair: reasonable, not at-entry. Staged entry — small

Macro fit

Research dossier: UBER — Uber Technologies, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-12 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 62

Next earnings: 2026-08-04

Business

Global demand-aggregation marketplace across Mobility (ride-hailing), Delivery (Eats/grocery),

and Freight. They take a cut of gross bookings routed through the network; riders/eaters and

drivers/couriers are the two sides, and Uber One (50M+ members, >50% of bookings) is the

loyalty/retention flywheel. The durability case is the marketplace: liquidity (riders ↔ drivers)

is a real two-sided moat that compounds with scale, and the same demand graph monetizes

whoever supplies the ride — including third-party autonomous fleets.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, tentatively. This is a fundamentally-strong, FCF-generative name that has been sold off

~33% from its high on a narrative (robotaxi disruption), not a numbers break. Q1 2026 (May 6)

was an acceleration print, not a deceleration:

  • Revenue $13.2B (+14% YoY); gross bookings $53.7B (+25%); Mobility +25%, Delivery +28%.
  • Non-GAAP EPS +44% YoY; Q2 guide $56.25–57.75B bookings (above consensus), EPS +31–38%.
  • Record $3B single-quarter buyback; Uber One 50M members (+50% YoY).

The stock popped ~10% on the print, then gave it all back and more as the AV-threat narrative

(Waymo, Tesla robotaxi, a Melius "Sell" call) reasserted. So the setup is: business inflecting

up, multiple inflecting down on a terminal-value debate. That divergence is the opportunity.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $68.77 (close 2026-06-12); market cap ~$140B; ~2.04B shares.
  • Forward P/E ~18.75; trailing P/E ~17 (trailing is on a depressed comp — 2025 net income

was inflated by one-time equity-stake/tax-valuation gains, hence net income "−30%" while

revenue rose +18%; the forward number is the cleaner read).

  • For a 25%-bookings / ~18%-revenue grower throwing off real FCF and buying back stock, forward

PEG is well under 1. That is cheap unless you believe the AV thesis structurally impairs the

mobility take rate.

  • Position vs. range: off_52w_high −32.6%, only +2.3% above the 52-week low, −6.4% vs

50DMA, −16.7% vs 200DMA, ret_60d −10.3%. This is a dip-buyer zone, the opposite of the

extended post-deal AI-infra field.

  • My fair-value read (conservative, haircutting for AV terminal risk): ~$85–95. Sell-side avg

PT ~$104 (Strong Buy, 51 analysts) — I'm below the Street on purpose. Current price sits

meaningfully under even my conservative fair value.

Post-deal / repriced check

Inverts cleanly: this is not a post-deal chase. There's no consumed entry edge here — the

opposite. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, UBER is "extended" in neither direction;

it's de-rated without a fundamentals break. The thing that changed in the last 7–14 days is

sentiment (robotaxi fear + a sell rating), not the business — which is precisely the setup

where the dip is information-light and the fundamentals still hold. The risk is that the AV

debate is a slow-burn terminal-value repricing, not a one-day overreaction.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: A 25%-bookings-growth marketplace at ~18x forward earnings, buying back stock at the

lows, where AVs route through Uber's demand graph (Waymo live on the app in Austin/Atlanta;

NVIDIA 100k-vehicle plan for 2027; Lucid/Nuro 20k+ by late 2026) — the moat is demand, not the car.

  • Bear: ~40% of mobility bookings are AV-exposed; if Waymo/Tesla scale direct-to-consumer,

Uber's take rate structurally compresses and today's "cheap" multiple is a value trap pricing a

declining terminal business.

  • First-principles: A profitable, FCF-positive demand aggregator at <19x forward earnings is a

reasonable business today; the AV question is a 2028–2030 debate, and you're being paid to wait.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived from this run's data; base-rate-aware (patterns.py: dip_buyer n=48, win 56% @ +21d

decaying to 49% @ +63d, avg +1.8% @ 63d, avg drawdown −14% / worst −45% — a coin-flip with a

Macro fit

Research dossier: VST — Vistra Corp.

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 62

Next earnings: 2026-08-06

Business

Largest US competitive (merchant) power producer + retail electricity. ~41 GW of generation

across nuclear (Comanche Peak TX; PJM fleet via Energy Harbor), natural gas, coal, solar/storage,

plus the TXU/Dynegy retail book that sells power to ~5M+ customers. Two ways they get paid:

(1) merchant energy + capacity margins on the fleet, (2) retail spread. The durable revenue is

the structural shortage of dispatchable baseload power against AI/data-center + electrification

demand — VST owns existing, already-interconnected, carbon-free nuclear that hyperscalers want

on 20-year contracts and cannot build themselves. This is the AI Energy Thesis / power-as-

binding-constraint lane, on the "owns the scarce already-permitted generation" side.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, on a de-rating, not a hype run. VST at $148 is ~33% off its 52-week high and ~13% below its

200-DMA — a power name that has been sold down into a dip-buyer zone, not chased up. Q1 2026

(reported 2026-05-07) was a record: revenue $5.64B (+43% YoY), Ongoing Adj EBITDA $1.494B, GAAP

net income $1.03B; full-year 2026 guidance reaffirmed at $6.8–7.6B Adj EBITDA and $3.9–4.7B

Adj FCFbG. The stock fell ~8% despite the beat — the overhang is regulatory (PJM capacity price

collar, below), not a demand or execution miss. Marquee contracts already signed: 20-yr Meta PPA

for 2,609 MW carbon-free from PJM nuclear, plus ~3,800 MW of nuclear PPAs with AWS. Cogentrix

acquisition (~5,500 MW gas, ~$3B) expected to close 2H 2026, adding dispatchable capacity into a

tight market. The setup is "real cash flow + secular tailwind + a fixable regulatory scare + a

genuine pullback" — the kind the macro read says to buy.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $148.02; market cap ~$49.9B; 337M shares. Trailing P/E ~25, forward P/E ~14

(consensus EPS growth running ~90% YoY into Q2). Renewable/IPP peer fwd P/E ~12, so VST sits at

a modest premium justified by the nuclear+AI-demand optionality.

  • EV/EBITDA: ~$50B equity + ~$13–15B net debt ≈ $63–65B EV / ~$7.2B 2026E Adj EBITDA (guide mid)

≈ ~9x forward EV/EBITDA — reasonable for a secular-demand power name, neither cheap nor rich.

  • My fair value: 9–10x 2026E EBITDA → ~$143 (9x) to ~$172 (10x) equity-per-share; call it

~$160–175 fair. Current $148 is modestly below my fair-value midpoint.

  • Range: 32.6% off 52w high, 11.6% above 52w low, ret_60d −13%, vs 50DMA −3.7%, vs 200DMA −13.4%.

Squarely a dip-buyer profile.

  • Street mean target ~$225–231 (50%+ upside) — I treat that as stale-bull anchoring and do NOT

underwrite it; my own fair value is well below the sell-side.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: the Meta and AWS nuclear PPAs already landed earlier in 2026 — so

the deals are in. But unlike the IREN-at-$61 trap, the multiple did NOT run after the deals; the

stock de-rated ~33% on the PJM regulatory scare. So this is "extended-WITHOUT-catalyst"

inverted: the entry edge was NOT consumed by an up-move — the post-deal repricing went down,

which reopens an entry rather than closing one. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, the

business fact that changed (the PJM collar) is a bearish fact already in the price, not a bull

catalyst I'd be chasing. Buying here is the opposite of the FOMO error.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Owns scarce, already-interconnected carbon-free nuclear under 20-yr hyperscaler PPAs;

$7B+ EBITDA at a 14x forward P/E into a multi-year power-demand supercycle is underpriced.

  • Bear: Merchant power is structurally price-capped now — the PJM $175–325/MW-day capacity

collar (FERC-approved through 2029/30) caps the very upside the bull case extrapolates, and a

~$13–15B debt load makes the equity rate-sensitive into a hawkish FOMC.

  • First-principles: A profitable, FCF-generative owner of irreplaceable baseload generation,

selling 33% off its high at 9x EBITDA with contracted demand growth — yes, that makes sense to

own from scratch at this price.

Macro fit

Research dossier: AVGO — Broadcom Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.58 · Opportunity score: 60

Next earnings: 2026-09-04

Business

Broadcom is two businesses fused: (1) Semiconductors (~$15B/qtr, the growth engine) — it

co-designs custom AI accelerators (XPUs) with individual hyperscalers and sells the

AI networking silicon (Tomahawk/Jericho switches, optics) that stitches thousands of those

chips into a cluster. Six core custom-silicon customers, now publicly including Google, Meta,

OpenAI and Anthropic. (2) Infrastructure software (~$7B/qtr, the cash ballast) — VMware plus

the legacy CA/Symantec stack, sold as sticky enterprise subscriptions. The AI accelerator + the

switch fabric is a genuine orchestration-vs-substrate substrate position: whoever builds

the cluster pays Broadcom regardless of which lab wins the model race. That's why the revenue

durably continues — it's picks-and-shovels at the silicon layer, and unlike the hyperscalers it

is FCF-positive right now, not underwater in the ai-capex-payback-window J-curve.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, conditionally. AVGO just fell ~18% over five sessions (close ~$387 on Jun 5, –7.5% that

day; SOX –6%) on its Jun 3 fiscal-Q2 print — and the print was good: revenue **$22.19B (+48%

YoY) beat the $22.13B consensus, non-GAAP EPS $2.44 beat $2.39, AI semi revenue $10.8B

grew +143% YoY, record adjusted EBITDA $15.2B (69% margin). The sell-off was a high-bar

miss, not a thesis crack: Q3 AI-semi guidance of $16.0B (+200% YoY)** landed below the ~$17.2B

whisper, infrastructure software grew only +9% ($7.18B) as VMware synergies mature, the CEO's

"chips-only" tone unnerved some holders, and the whole semi cohort de-rated into the FOMC.

Management reaffirmed (did not raise) ~$56B FY2026 AI revenue (~180% growth) and **reiterated

$100B FY2027 AI revenue**. So this is precisely the extended-with-vs-without-catalyst frame

inverted: not an extended chase, but a real dip on a quality name where the secular numbers are

still accelerating. Base rates support buying this shape: AVGO's own dip-buyer history wins

70% at +21d (avg +3.6%) and 60% at +63d (avg +10.8%), with avg drawdown −10.7% (worst

−29.7%) — so entry should be staged, not all-at-market.

$29.3B (+127%). P/S ~24× TTM.

still +6.7% vs 200DMA.

AVGO is not a Wait-for-deal thesis small-cap — there is no single pending lab partnership

opposite direction: the multiple just de-rated ~18% on a guidance disappointment. The

entry edge was restored, not consumed. The honest caution is that AVGO is a consensus

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: A FCF-positive AI-substrate monopoly growing accelerator revenue +143% with FY27

>$100B reiterated, now 23% off its high at a sub-1 PEG — the quality way to own the buildout.

  • Bear: Six-customer concentration + a guide that missed the whisper + software decelerating

  • First-principles: At $1.82T for ~$30B of growing net income compounding 100%+, the price

Researched: 2026-06-11 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 60

Business

Largest US electricity producer post-Calpine. ~32GW of generation, anchored by the country's

The Microsoft/Crane Clean Energy Center (Three Mile Island Unit 1, 835MW, 20-yr PPA, restart

Research dossier: NRG — NRG Energy, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-10 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 60

Business

NRG is an integrated independent power producer (IPP) + competitive retail energy supplier —

the largest competitive retailer in the US, heavily weighted to Texas/ERCOT, plus a generation

fleet that roughly doubled to ~25 GW after closing the **$12B LS Power acquisition (Jan 30,

2026)**. Revenue comes from (1) selling power/retail energy plans to homes and businesses and

(2) wholesale generation. The new growth leg is selling firm power to AI data centers: a

project-development agreement with **GE Vernova + Kiewit/TIC to build up to 5.4 GW of new gas

generation** (1.2 GW by 2029, 1.2 GW by 2030, ~3 GW 2030–32), ~445 MW already contracted, and

the company openly negotiating ~7.5 GW more. Why revenue durably continues: power demand is

structurally rising (electrification + AI load), NRG owns dispatchable generation and the #1

retail book in the fastest-growing power market, and gas turbine equipment is supply-constrained

through ~2030 — NRG has already secured slots.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, conditionally. This is the rare name in the field that is **cheap, profitable, in a

library-favored lane, and NOT post-deal-repriced** — it has de-rated, not run. It sits at its

52-week low (~$120.6 vs a $189.96 high, −36%) trading at ~12–13.5× forward earnings with

$2.8–3.3B of FCF-before-growth guided. Per AI Energy Thesis, power is the binding AI

constraint and behind-the-meter / firm gas generation is the dominant 2026 lane — NRG is a

direct, cash-generative expression of that thesis rather than a speculative pre-revenue infra

name. The catch: the recent collapse is idiosyncratic (a Q1 earnings miss + a month of

downward estimate revisions), which macro-cohort-confirmation treats as *information, not a

liquidity dip*. The mitigant is that the miss cause — mild Texas weather and low power-price

volatility in a seasonally back-half-weighted business — is transient, and **full-year guidance

was reaffirmed**. So it's a falling knife on relatively benign news: worth buying, but

trigger-gated, not chased at-market while estimates are still being cut.

Research dossier: HPE — Hewlett Packard Enterprise Co.

Researched: 2026-06-12 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.60 · Opportunity score: 58

Next earnings: 2026-09-03

Business

HPE is a ~$39B-TTM-revenue enterprise IT infrastructure company, now structured around three pillars post-Juniper:

1. Server / AI compute — ProLiant Gen12, Cray-class AI systems, and the new NVIDIA Vera-CPU DL394 Gen12 for agentic-AI workloads. Sells to hyperscalers, sovereign clouds, neoclouds, and enterprise. The growth engine right now, but the lowest-margin pillar — record AI-server backlog drives revenue, not margin.

2. Networking (Juniper, $14B deal) — Aruba campus + Mist AI + data-center switching/optical interconnect. The AI training-fabric play (the switching inside GPU clusters). Higher-margin and the synergy story; cost synergies are running ahead of schedule.

3. Hybrid cloud / GreenLake — consumption-model ARR layered on hardware; the recurring-revenue, margin-accretive pillar over time.

Blended gross margin is structurally far above pure ODMs (HPE captures networking + software alongside boxes), but net margin is thin (~3.7% TTM) because the revenue mix is currently tilted to low-margin AI servers. The EPS-leverage thesis rests on mix-shift toward Juniper networking and GreenLake as the AI-server wave runs through. Revenue durability is the multi-year AI buildout (capex kill-switch far from tripping) plus sovereign/enterprise AI procurement that lags hyperscalers and so has a longer runway.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The prior dossier (2026-06-05) landed HOLD at $53 with an explicit watch case: flip to BUY on a pullback to $44–47. That pullback has essentially arrived. After the Q2 blowout (June 1) the stock spiked to $64.25 on June 2, then bled back ~25% to close $48.17 on 2026-06-12 (it printed $49.20 on a -8.4% Friday in a -2.6% S&P tape). The watch case the last run wrote down is the setup we now have.

What makes this an entry case rather than chasing:

  • Q2 FY2026 was a genuine blowout: revenue $10.68B (+40% YoY), non-GAAP EPS $0.79 vs. ~$0.54 consensus (~46% beat), strong FCF. FY26 guide raised to +29–33% revenue ($44.2–45.6B), non-GAAP EPS $3.35–3.45, FCF >$3.5B; Q3 revenue guided $11.5–12.1B. A FY2027 growth framework was introduced. Neri: "strongest AI-server backlog we have ever seen." H3C stake exit (~$3.5B cumulative proceeds) cleaned the balance sheet.
  • Valuation is undemanding for the cohort: forward P/E ~12–14× (stockanalysis forward 12.15×) on a name guiding 30%+ revenue growth — a sub-1 forward PEG. This is the cheap, real-FCF AI-infra name, not an extreme-P/S story.
  • Near-term catalyst: HPE Discover / Investor Relations Summit June 16 (Neri keynote "Architecting AI starts with your network" + IR strategy session) — a fresh catalyst, which is exactly the macro-stance gate ("buy real dips OR fresh catalysts").

The honest caveat: the 25% "dip" is off a 10-day-old spike, not a return to value — HPE still sits ~+86% above its 200DMA and the screen tags it extended_run. The entry edge is the valuation + dip + catalyst combination, not a washed-out chart.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $48.17; market cap ~$63.8B; shares ~1.32B; TTM revenue $38.79B; net margin ~3.7%; trailing P/E 43.7× (depressed by pre-turn earnings), forward P/E ~12–14×.
  • 52-week range: ~25% off the $64.25 high; ~175% above the 52-week low — the post-turn re-rate has been violent.
  • My fair value: ~16× FY26 EPS $3.40 ≈ $54–56, with optionality toward consensus $64 (22 analysts, avg $64.13, ~33% implied upside) if the FY27 framework + Juniper synergies convince the sell side the growth is durable. At $48 the market pays ~14× for 30% growth — I read that as ~10–15% undervalued vs. my base case, more if mix-shift lifts margins.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis, the Juniper deal and the Q2 print are both consumed — the entry edge is no longer "wait for the catalyst." This fits the immediate-compression-then-digestion pattern: the news spiked the stock to $64 in 48 hours (over-shoot), and it has since bled back as the move was parsed. Per the framework, the follow-on entry comes from a normal dip, not the deal itself — which is precisely the $64→$48 give-back we're buying. So the old bull case (Juniper + AI servers) is NOT the entry case; the entry case is the post-overshoot price plus an undemanding multiple. That distinction is what moves this from the prior HOLD to a cautious BUY.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: 30%+ revenue growth, record AI backlog, Juniper synergies ahead of plan, FCF >$3.5B — and you pay ~14× forward, the cheapest real-cash-flow name in the AI-infra cohort.

Independent power producer (IPP) in PJM. Crown jewel is the ~2.2 GW Susquehanna nuclear plant

in PA; the rest is a merchant gas/coal fleet, recently enlarged by the Cornerstone

acquisition of two CCGT gas plants (financed April 2026). Who pays them: wholesale power

markets (spark spreads + PJM capacity payments) and, increasingly, a hyperscaler under a

long-dated retail PPA. The durability case is the AI Energy Thesis nuclear lane — Talen

sells dispatchable, carbon-free baseload into a grid where AI data-center load is the marginal

buyer, and the Susquehanna footprint is already-permitted, already-interconnected (long the

permitting-as-binding-constraint). The June-2025 Amazon deal: 1.9 GW, 17-year, ~$18B

revenue, restructured from co-located behind-the-meter into a grid-connected front-of-meter

retail PPA (Talen as AWS's licensed PA retail provider), plus optionality on SMRs and

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Q1 2026 (reported May 5): Adjusted EBITDA $473M (>2× YoY), Adjusted FCF $350M (~4× YoY), driven

by the acquisitions + tighter PJM spark spreads. 2026 guidance reaffirmed: Adj EBITDA

$1.75–2.05B, Adj FCF $0.98–1.18B (ex-Cornerstone); pro-forma EBITDA tracks toward ~$2B with

Cornerstone. $1.9B buyback authorization runs through 2028 (~12% of the cap; $100M bought in

Q1). So the operating story is intact and improving. The catch is timing, not quality: unlike

the rest of the AI-infra cohort, TLN is not extended — ret_60d +6.5%, ret_20d +2.2%, sitting

right on its 50-DMA (+0.7%) and below its 200-DMA (−2.9%), ~20% off the 52w high. That is a

consolidating, mildly-corrective post-deal name, not a parabola. The fresh-buy question is

therefore a value + dip question, not a momentum-chase. The honest answer: it's a high-quality

name at a fair-to-slightly-cheap price, but the entry-defining catalyst is a year old and the

near-term macro (binary FOMC in 3 days, beta 1.60) argues for a trigger-gated entry rather than

  • Price ~$360.58. Market cap ~$16.4B on ~45.4M shares. 52w range $255.50–$451.28 (20% off high,

Post-deal / repriced check

Research dossier: CIEN — Ciena Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-16 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.68 · Opportunity score: 50

Next earnings: 2026-09-03

Business

Ciena designs and sells optical networking hardware, software, and services. The flagship is

WaveLogic coherent optics (the DSP-driven modems that push data down long-haul and data-center

fiber — 800G shipping, 1.6T qualifying), plus packet/routing platforms and a network-management

software layer. The newest product is RLS Hyper-Rail, an intelligent line system co-created

for AI-training interconnect across hundreds of kilometers.

Who pays them: historically Tier-1 carriers (AT&T, Verizon, BT), but the growth has fully

rotated to hyperscalers building data-center interconnect (DCI) — the fiber links between

AI data centers carrying petabits/second. Ciena is the incumbent leader, with proprietary DSP

silicon and 20+ years of deployment density. Revenue durability rests on: (a) 7–10-year optical

equipment lifecycles; (b) the operational catastrophe of switching optical vendors mid-build;

(c) a hyperscaler capex wave still in its early-middle phase. This is a substrate bet —

picks-and-shovels for the AI buildout, paid every time a hyperscaler adds a data center

(orchestration-vs-substrate).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The AI-DCI thesis is real and executing, and the stock is now ~$207 (32%) off its $637 high.

Q2 FY2026 (reported June 4, qtr ended ~May 2): revenue $1.57B, +40% YoY (Optical +42%,

Routing & Switching +88%), backlog $7.7B, and the **industry-first multi-rail hyperscaler

"Hyper-Rail" order** (initial multi-hundred-million contract, rollouts beginning 2027, plus

"discussions with multiple additional hyperscalers, neoscalers and service providers"). The

business is firing.

MDBHOLDfetched50›

Research dossier: MDB — MongoDB, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.58 · Opportunity score: 50

Next earnings: 2026-08-27

Business

MongoDB sells a document-oriented database platform. Two revenue buckets:

  • Atlas (cloud-managed, consumption-based) — the growth engine and now ~75% of total

revenue (up from 72% a year ago), growing 29.4% YoY in Q1 FY2027 and adding a record $117M

of dollar net-new ARR in a single quarter. Multi-cloud, serverless, with vector search,

text/time-series search, and stream processing folded in natively — the bet is collapsing a

separate vector store + operational DB + feature store into one managed surface, and becoming

the persistent-state layer for AI agents going to production.

  • Enterprise Advanced + Other (self-managed license + support) — mature, +13% YoY, ARR

~+11%, driven by existing finance/tech customers. High-margin, declining share of mix.

Who pays: dev-centric enterprises building modern apps — fintech, e-commerce, gaming, real-time

personalization, AI-agent backends. The document model's schema flexibility is the natural fit

when data shape evolves fast (i.e. every AI product in production). Atlas is sticky once data

pipelines are built on it. Durability: high.

Q1 FY2027 (quarter ended 2026-04-30): revenue $687.6M (+25% YoY) — an acceleration from the

~22% trough — non-GAAP op margin 18% (up from 16%), non-GAAP EPS $1.32 (vs $1.18 est.),

net income positive. TTM revenue $2.60B (+23.6%).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

**The Q1 beat-and-raise was a real, thesis-confirming catalyst — and the stock has now given

back the entire post-earnings gap. The setup is the best it's looked in months, but the timing

is poor.**

The sequence matters. MDB ran from ~$308 in mid-May to its $444.72 52-week high

the day after the 2026-05-28 print, then faded all the way back to ~$343 by 2026-06-13. That is

not the thesis weakening — it's a macro-driven round-trip (high-P/S software de-risking into the

FOMC). Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: the fact that changed in the last ~2 weeks is

real (growth re-accelerated to 25%, Atlas +29% with record net-new ARR, op margin expanding,

FY2027 guide raised). So this is repricing, not FOMO — and unusually, the price has handed the

repricing back. You can now own the stronger thesis at roughly the pre-catalyst price.

That is exactly the "real dip into a confirmed catalyst" that the macro read carves out as a

valid exception to "hold cash." So why not a clean BUY? Three live counterweights:

1. The base rate says don't buy this name's dips at-market. patterns.py (dip_buyer preset

active today): +63d win 33.3%, avg −13.5%, avg max drawdown −25.2% across 32

instances. MDB dips have historically kept dipping. This doesn't veto a buy, but it demands a

trigger-gated entry below current, not a chase at the tick.

2. The FOMC dot plot is 3 days out (June 16–17). The macro read flags this as the swing

event for high-P/S names — an easing-bias→neutral shift or "no cuts until 2027" pressures

them immediately. MDB at a forward P/E ~54 (non-GAAP) and ~9.4× forward sales is "moderate"

tier, not extreme like NBIS/IREN, but it is not immune. Buying at-market 3 days before a

binary macro de-rate event is poor sequencing.

3. Structural substitution bear is unresolved — AWS DocumentDB, Azure Cosmos, Google

AlloyDB offer Mongo-compatible APIs at hyperscaler economics.

Bottom line: I want to own MDB and the entry has finally come back to me. But the empirical

dip base rate + a binary FOMC inside 72 hours say gate it, don't chase it. On the doorstep,

not through the door at-market.

Valuation + position vs. range

~$273/sh.

Post-deal / repriced check

Not a "wait-for-deal" name (product company, not infra-for-hire). The relevant question: has the

MUHOLDfetched50›

Research dossier: MU — Micron Technology

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.60 · Opportunity score: 50

Next earnings: 2026-06-24

Business

Micron is one of three global DRAM/NAND makers (with SK Hynix and Samsung). DRAM is ~75% of

revenue — server/data-center DRAM and, increasingly, High Bandwidth Memory (HBM); NAND ~20%.

The business is capital-intensive and historically the most cyclical semiconductor vertical:

oligopoly pricing swings the P&L violently. The structural change of 2025–26 is HBM as a

discrete, high-ASP sub-market driven by GPU training/inference demand — HBM3E (H100/H200) and

HBM4 (Blackwell/Vera Rubin) carry 3–5× standard-DRAM ASPs. Micron is #3 in HBM (~21% share vs

SK Hynix ~62%, Samsung ~17%) but is the swing supplier NVIDIA wants for HBM4 dual-sourcing.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The AI-memory supercycle is running hotter than my last read (2026-06-05) modeled, and — the key

tell — the stock rose from ~$900 to ~$982 while getting cheaper on forward earnings. That is

earnings catching up to price, not multiple expansion.

What changed in the last 7–14 days (this is extended WITH catalyst, per

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, not FOMO):

  • Sell-side repricing, June 1–3: PTs roughly doubled — Raymond James $530→$1,100, Wolfe

$550→$1,250, UBS $535→$1,625 (Street high), Susquehanna $600→$1,750. The FY2027 earnings

outlook was revised sharply upward across the board.

  • Memory pricing surge: TrendForce sees conventional DRAM contract prices +55–60% in Q1'26,

NAND +33–38%; HBM4 priced >50% above HBM3E; HBM capacity for all of 2026 sold out and price/

volume agreements finalized. HBM TAM now seen reaching ~$100B by 2028 (from $35B in 2025) —

pulled in ~two years vs prior guidance.

So the question the framework forces is the right one: *is the new thesis worth owning at the new

price?* On forward earnings — yes, the valuation is attractive. On entry timing — **no, this

is a poor spot to buy at-market**: the stock sits ~9.9% off its $1,089 ATH (set Jun 3, i.e. the

catalyst already fired and the stock is slightly below its post-catalyst peak), and two binaries

land inside 11 days — the June 16–17 FOMC dot plot (3 days out) and the June 24 Q3 print.

Verdict: HOLD — but a warm hold, materially more constructive than the June 5 HOLD. Cheap on

forward earnings with a real, recent catalyst, but I will not buy at-market into a double-binary

in a hold-cash macro. A Q3 beat-with-Q4-guide-up (cycle not peaking) OR a macro/FOMC-driven dip

toward ~$850 converts this to BUY.

Valuation + position vs. range

**Fair-value derivation (re-derived this run — prior $700 buy-below / $70–85 FY27 EPS is stale

and too low given the upward earnings revision):** central FY2027 EPS ~$90–100, bull ~$115–120,

bear (cycle turns) ~$50. At 11–12× central ~$95 → $1,045–1,140 fair. At $982 the stock trades

below my fair-value range on forward earnings. The catch: those are peak-cycle earnings, and

10× peak earnings is exactly what memory looks like right before a turn. The cheapness is real;

so is the value-trap risk.

Post-deal / repriced check

MU is not a Wait-for-deal thesis name — there is no single deal to consume the edge; it's a

cyclical riding an earnings supercycle. The 830%+ run has happened, but unlike a one-shot deal,

the earnings are still accelerating sequentially ($23.86B Q2 → ~$33.5B Q3 guide → ~$34.4B

consensus), which is why the forward multiple compressed to ~10× even as the price rose. This is a

different shape from the IREN-at-$61 mistake (paying up for a post-deal name ~30% above entry with

no fresh catalyst): here a fresh catalyst (PT doublings + pricing surge) did land, and the

multiple is contracting, not expanding. The discipline that still applies: don't confuse "cheap on

peak earnings" with margin of safety, and don't buy the pre-binary spot just because the forward

P/E is low.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: 10× forward earnings with HBM sold out through 2026, DRAM contract prices +55–60%, and

the Street doubling targets — this is a secular re-rate the market is still underpricing on a

forward basis.

  • Bear: It's the #3 player in the most cyclical semi business on earth at 10× peak earnings;

supply discipline always breaks, and when SK Hynix/Samsung normalize, margins and EPS halve

inside 12 months — "cheap" becomes "value trap."

  • First-principles: A commodity-memory maker at a $1.1T cap on a one-year earnings spike is a

bet that this cycle's supply discipline is structurally different (HBM tying up wafer capacity)

rather than the usual oligopoly cheating — plausible, but it's a cycle-durability bet, not a

free lunch.

Entry levels the agent derives

  • Buy-below: ~$850 — ~22% off the $1,089 high, ~9× my central FY27 EPS; where a genuine dip

and a forward-earnings margin of safety align. (Raised from the stale $700; earnings revised up.)

  • Fair: $1,045–1,140 — 11–12× central FY27 EPS (~$95). Current $982 is below this band.
  • Stretch: $1,500–1,700 — ~14× bull-case EPS (~$115); aligns with the UBS $1,625 /

Susquehanna $1,750 Street-high targets. Earned if Q3/Q4 confirm the cycle extends into 2027–28.

Macro fit

Kill-switches (per state/research/macro.md, 2026-06-13): all three clear and far from

threshold — capex +77% YoY, GPU pricing/lead-times firm (B200 not compressing), HY OAS ~275bp.

Secular demand floor intact; MU is a direct beneficiary (more GPUs → more HBM per accelerator).

Regime overlay: market extended (fwd P/E ~20–22×), breadth narrow, and the **FOMC dot plot is

3–4 sessions out** — macro stance is *hold cash into the FOMC; only real dips or fresh catalysts;

small size on high-P/S*. MU is less exposed to dot-plot multiple-compression than NBIS/IREN (it

cyclical that sells off hard in a risk-off dot-plot. Buying at-market, near highs, pre-FOMC and

Business

AI-security revenue nearly doubled. Yet the stock fell — because management did not raise

nothing here forces the discount to close.

Research dossier: MSFT — Microsoft Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 48

Next earnings: 2026-07-29

Business

Three segments: Productivity & Business Processes (M365/Office, Copilot add-on, LinkedIn,

Dynamics), Intelligent Cloud (Azure, server products, enterprise services), and More Personal

Computing (Windows, Xbox, search). Who pays: enterprises on multi-year commercial contracts

(the commercial backlog is the durability signal), consumers on subscriptions, and — increasingly

— AI workloads on Azure. Revenue durably continues because of switching costs (M365 is embedded

in enterprise workflow), the Azure enterprise distribution moat, and now the OpenAI compute

relationship that routes a structurally large AI workload through Azure. TTM revenue $318B

(+17.9%), TTM EPS $16.79 (+29.8%), net income $125B — the strongest cash base of any hyperscaler.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The interesting fact today is that MSFT is not extended — it's the rare megacap AI name

sitting in a genuine dip. At $390.67 it's 29.6% off its 52-week high ($555.45), only 9.6%

above its 52-week low ($356.28), and -13.9% vs its 200DMA / -5.1% vs 50DMA. The de-rate is

real and earned a "worst quarter since 2008" headline: despite a Q3 beat (EPS $4.27 vs $4.06,

Azure +39% cc above guide), the market punished (1) margin compression — gross margin 67.6%, the

narrowest since 2022, as data-center depreciation mounts and AI-services GM (~60%) drags the ~80%

software blend; (2) $190B FY26 capex guidance (+61% YoY, ~$25B of it component-cost inflation);

(3) FQ4 operating-margin guide ticking to 44% from 46.3%; and (4) the structural "AI will eat

software" fear plus "the payback is too far away."

Read through the J-curve frame (ai-capex-payback-window), this is the textbook

case: FCF/margin compression while capex guidance is still rising is the expected shape,

not a thesis break. Kill-switch #1 (capex <10% YoY for two cycles) is nowhere near tripped — MSFT

is raising. So the market is selling the symptom (margin compression) that the framework says

to expect. That disconnect is the bull case. The caution: "expected shape" doesn't mean the

de-rate is over — the overhang (when does $190B/yr pay back?) is unresolved and the market can

keep paying a lower multiple for years while it waits.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Forward P/E ~21.1×, trailing 23.3×. That is roughly **in line with the S&P's ~20–22×

forward** (macro.md) — for a name compounding EPS ~30% with the best balance sheet in the

cohort. MSFT has historically commanded a premium to the index; it has now given that premium

back. That's the repricing, and it's the most important valuation fact: this is no longer an

"extreme-P/S" name exposed to the FOMC multiple-compression risk the macro read flags for

NBIS/IREN. P/S ~9.1× (mkt cap $2.90T / TTM rev $318B).

  • Fair-value read: at ~21× forward on a 15–18% revenue grower with margin headwinds, the

stock is fair, not cheap. The Street's $561 target (43% "upside," 52 Buy / 3 Hold / 0 Sell)

read: fair value sits ~$390–420 today; a genuinely cheap entry needs a retest toward the

$356–375 low, where forward P/E compresses toward ~20× or below.

  • Position vs range: bottom third of the 52w range — the opposite of the chase-the-high problem.

Post-deal / repriced check

Not a "wait-for-deal" small-cap, but the analogous check applies to the **restructured OpenAI

partnership** (the would-be fresh catalyst): ~$250B Azure commitment + IP rights through 2032,

Wait-for-deal thesis the catalyst edge is consumed: I can't treat the OpenAI restructure

Business

Two engines under one holdco. FPL (Florida Power & Light) — the largest US regulated

electric utility by retail sales, a low-cost, rate-base-growth monopoly serving ~12M Floridians;

the steady, credit-quality cash flow. NextEra Energy Resources (NEER) — the largest developer

of wind/solar/storage in the world, ~33 GW renewables+storage backlog, increasingly the

unregulated growth engine selling power into the AI/data-center load surge. Newer legs: a DoC

award to build 9.5 GW of gas for large loads in TX/PA, plus a 30-GW data-center "hub"

ambition by 2035. Revenue durably continues because both legs are long the single biggest

secular demand story in US power — data-center load growth that the grid physically can't keep up

with (AI Energy Thesis).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The whole question right now is the Dominion acquisition (announced 2026-05-18). NEE is buying

Dominion in a $66.8B all-stock deal — 0.8138 NEE shares per D share plus a pro-rata slice of a

$360M cash payment, ~23% premium to D's pre-announcement close, largest utility merger on record,

expected to close mid-to-late 2027. NEE has slid ~10% over 20 days on the announcement.

The strategic logic is genuinely strong, not an empire grab: Dominion owns Data Center Alley

(Northern Virginia, the world's largest data-center market) — data centers were ~28% of its

Virginia sales last year and it carries a ~51 GW contracted data-center pipeline. Combined

large-load pipeline ~130 GW. And per Bloomberg the deal is credit-accretive: NEE had been

leaning on unregulated NEER earnings, drifting toward downgrade thresholds; bolting on Dominion's

regulated VA rate base shores up the credit profile. So the sell-off is the mechanical all-stock

dilution trade-down that acquirers reliably take on signing — not the market judging the deal

value-destructive.

But that does not make it a buy here for the marginal $100. The drop is name-specific (this

deal), not a cohort-wide dip — it fails the macro-cohort-confirmation test for dip-buying.

The deal injects ~18 months of regulatory binary (FERC + Virginia SCC + DOJ on the

largest-ever utility merger; VA SCC + data-center-rate politics is a live concession/break risk),

which caps re-rating until clarity lands. And the timing is poor: the **FOMC dot plot is 3 days

out (Jun 16-17)** and NEE is a rate-sensitive bond-proxy (2.9% yield) — exactly the kind of name a

hawkish "no cuts until 2027" dot pressures. Quality name, real pullback, wrong moment to commit at

market.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $85.99; mkt cap ~$179B; trailing P/E 21.8×, forward P/E ~21.2×; div yield 2.90%

($2.49); EPS $3.94; revenue $27.9B TTM; ~2.09B shares. 52-wk range $67.20–$98.75; ~13% off

the high, ~28% above the low, sitting right on its 200DMA (-5.7% vs 50DMA after the deal slide).

  • Guidance: 2026 adj EPS $3.92–$4.02 (targeting high end); reaffirmed **≥8% adj-EPS CAGR

through 2032 and 2035** off the 2025 base of $3.71. Q1'26 adj EPS $1.09 beat $0.97 (+10% YoY).

  • My fair-value read: an 8%+ regulated-quality grower with a real AI-power tailwind earns

~20–22× forward EPS; on ~$3.97 that's ~$79–$87 before the deal overhang. Applying a

deal-uncertainty/dilution discount, fair ≈ $84 — i.e. roughly where it trades. The ~10%

($98.47 avg, BMO $104) price the deal as cleanly accretive and the regulatory path as smooth,

which I think is too optimistic on timing.

Post-deal / repriced check

Different flavor of "deal" than Wait-for-deal thesis (which is about small/mid infra

pre-lab-partnership). Here NEE is the acquirer and the catalyst is dilutive M&A. The entry

edge is not consumed in the usual sense — if anything the stock is cheaper post-announcement —

but the name is now a special situation: an 18-month merger-arb-flavored overhang sits on top

of the operating story. You're no longer buying a clean compounder; you're buying a compounder

plus a regulatory binary that won't resolve until 2027. Size and timing must respect that.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: A mechanical dilution dip on a credit-accretive deal that hands NEE the world's

premier data-center grid (51 GW VA pipeline) — the cleanest scaled way to own AI power demand.

  • Bear: All-stock 25.5% dilution for an 18-month regulatory slog (Virginia SCC + FERC + DOJ on

  • First-principles: At 21× forward for an 8% grower yielding 2.9%, with a rate-cut-dependent

  • fair: $84 (deal-discounted fair value; roughly current).
  • stretch: $92 (justified only on deal-path clarity and a dovish/neutral FOMC).

Macro fit
