ONTOHOLDfetched38›

Research dossier: ONTO — Onto Innovation Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-12 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.62 · Opportunity score: 38

Next earnings: 2026-08-06

Business

Onto Innovation sells process-control tools to chipmakers — optical metrology, macro/inspection

(the Dragonfly line), lithography systems, and software — used to measure and find defects across

wafer fab and, increasingly, advanced packaging. Customers are the logic foundries (TSMC), the

HBM memory makers (SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron), and OSATs. The durable revenue driver is structural,

not cyclical product cycles: AI accelerators are bottlenecked on **advanced packaging (CoWoS / 2.5D

logic) and HBM stacking**, and every additional stacked-die / hybrid-bond step adds inspection and

metrology insertions. ONTO is a picks-and-shovels play on the part of the AI buildout that is the

actual physical constraint — and it sells to all the memory/foundry competitors rather than betting

on one. The recent TSMC qualification of Dragonfly G5 for AI packaging lines makes ONTO a default

tool supplier on the most advanced 2.5D logic flows — a real moat-deepening event.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business thesis is strong and the entry thesis is weak. Q1'26 revenue was $292M (beat its

own guide on margin), Q2 guided $320–330M (~consensus $325M), and management reaffirmed **FY26 >$1.3B

(>30% growth) with advanced packaging up >50%**. The TSMC G5 qual + June G5 shipments + Deutsche

Bank initiation (Buy, $350) and B. Riley ($365) drove a +60% run in 60 days. Per

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, this is **extended with a fresh catalyst** — repricing, not

pure FOMO — so the right question is "is the new thesis worth owning at the new price?" My answer: the

thesis is worth owning, but at $323.64 — sitting at the 52-week high, +69% vs the 200DMA — the

catalyst is largely consumed in price. The published analyst targets ($350/$365) are only ~8–13%

above spot; the easy money has been made.

Valuation + position vs. range

Spot $323.64; market cap ~$16.1B; 52-week range $89.40 – $326.96 — it trades within 1% of the high

and is +262% off the low. TTM revenue $1.03B (+0.5%, a trough year) with TTM net income ~$106M, so

trailing P/E ~150× is a trough-earnings artifact and not the relevant number. The forward picture:

forward P/E ~40.5×, ~12× forward P/S on the >$1.3B guide. That is a clear **premium to the semicap

group** (KLAC ~30× fwd, LRCX ~25×, AMAT ~22×). The premium is defensible — ONTO is the

highest-growth, purest advanced-packaging/HBM-levered name with a fresh TSMC default-supplier win — but

40× forward fully prices continued flawless execution, and the trough-to-recovery operating leverage

cuts both ways: one guide-down compresses the multiple and the earnings simultaneously. Much of the

2026 recovery is already in the tape.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: yes, the entry edge has largely been consumed. The qual win, the June

shipments, and the bullish initiations are the "deal," and the stock has run 60% into them to its 52-week

high. This is the textbook transition the Opportunity-cost lens flags — a name that was a great buy

before the catalyst becomes a marginal buy after the stock reprices. The old bull case ("ONTO is

the advanced-packaging bottleneck play") is true but is no longer an entry case at $323.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The single purest listed bet on the AI buildout's actual bottleneck (HBM/2.5D packaging),

now a TSMC default supplier, guiding >50% packaging growth — 40× forward is cheap for that durability.

  • Bear: A trough-earnings cyclical at the 52-week high and 40× forward, +69% over its 200DMA, where

one guide-down compresses multiple and EPS together — and you're paying up days before a binary FOMC.

  • First-principles: A great franchise at a full price bought at exactly the wrong moment is a

mediocre trade; the business deserves ownership, this entry does not.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived this run from forward ~$8.0 EPS (implied by 40.5× at $323.88) and the DMA structure

(50DMA ≈ $273, 200DMA ≈ $191):

  • Buy-below: $270 — a real pullback to the 50DMA zone (~17% off) and multiple compression toward

~34× forward; this is where the extended-run base rate stops fighting the entry.

  • Fair: $295 — ~37× forward; own-it-on-quality but no margin of safety.
  • Stretch: $330 — only on a fresh catalyst (formal HBM4 design win, a guide-raise), not at-market.

Entry-shape base rates (patterns.py, extended_run preset, 24 instances): +42d win 75%, +63d win

68%, avg +5.8% at 63d, but avg drawdown -13.4% (worst -49%). Momentum continuation is real but

the average payoff is modest against the drawdown — confirming a trigger-gated dip entry, not at-market.

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear and supportive of ONTO's demand: hyperscaler capex +77% YoY and still being

raised (kill-switch #1 far from tripped), Blackwell sold out with 6–12mo lead times — the buildout that

feeds advanced-packaging tool demand is intact. But the regime overlay vetoes the entry: macro stance

(2026-06-12) is *hold cash into the June 16–17 FOMC, buy only real dips or fresh catalysts, small size on

high-P/S names*. ONTO is a ~12× P/S name at its 52-week high, 4 days ahead of a binary dot-plot that

specifically pressures extreme-multiple names. On the Opportunity-cost lens, the marginal dollar is

poorly spent here at-market — middling cross-sectional rank: high business quality, but consumed entry

edge and an adverse macro window. Hence opportunity score 38, not higher.

Falsification

  • 30-day (flips toward BUY): a real give-back to the $270 buy-below zone without a thesis crack, or

the FOMC dot plot landing dovish/benign (high-P/S sizing constraint loosens).

  • 60-day: Q2 print (Aug 6) — a guide-raise on advanced packaging / a named HBM4 design win re-arms

the catalyst and justifies paying up; an in-line-or-soft guide at 40× forward is the give-back trigger.

  • 90-day (flips toward AVOID): any sign of advanced-packaging/HBM order digestion, semicap multiple

de-rate on a leadership-cohort sell-off, or kill-switch #1 (hyperscaler capex guidance) decelerating —

ONTO's 40× forward has no cushion for a cohort de-rate.

WDCHOLDfetched37›

Research dossier: WDC — Western Digital Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-15 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 37

Next earnings: 2026-07-29

Business

Post-SanDisk separation (Feb 2025), WDC is now a pure-play hard-disk-drive maker — no

NAND/flash. It sells high-capacity nearline HDDs into hyperscaler data centers, and that is

essentially the whole company now: Q3 FY26 cloud revenue was 89% of sales (+48% YoY to $3.0B),

driven by hyperscaler nearline demand. Revenue durability rests on one thing: storing the

exhaust of the AI buildout (model checkpoints, training corpora, inference logs, video) is

cheapest on spinning disk, and HDD supply is structurally tight — the industry consolidated to

~3 players (WDC, Seagate, Toshiba) and has not added capacity, so AI-era data growth meets a

fixed supply curve. That's the pricing-power engine. Next-gen capacity (HAMR 44TB / EPMR 40TB,

now in qualification with 4 customers, roadmap >100TB) is the durability layer if it ships.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business is firing on every cylinder, but not at this price/timing. The thesis is

real and improving — this is "extended with a catalyst" (per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst),

i.e. repricing, not pure FOMO. Q3 FY26 (reported Apr 30): revenue $3.3B (+45% YoY), non-GAAP

EPS $2.72 (~2×), gross margin 50.5% (+1,040 bps YoY, +440 bps QoQ). Q4 guide: revenue

$3.65B ±100M (+40% YoY), GM 51–52%, EPS $3.25 ±0.15. Margins this high for a historically

~30% cyclical commodity business is the tell that the shortage is doing real work.

But the entry is the problem, not the company. The stock is up ~16% today alone, tripled

in 2026 (~$187 → ~$653), sits 0.8% off its 52-week high, +154% vs its 200DMA, and now trades

above the average analyst price target ($547 — ~16% downside) even after JPM/BofA/Wells

Fargo raised targets to $650/$610/$575. Buying at-market here is paying up at the top of a

parabolic move, above where the sell-side that upgraded it thinks it should be, one day

before the FOMC dot plot. That is chasing, not entering.

Valuation + position vs. range

~$653, market cap ~$225B, 344.7M shares. TTM revenue $11.78B (+32%), TTM net income $6.38B

(+296%). Trailing P/E ~30.8; forward P/E ~30–36 (sources disagree; GAAP TTM EPS $18.35 is

inflated by separation/one-time items, so trailing understates the true multiple). On a clean

forward non-GAAP run-rate (~$13–14 annualized off the $3.25 Q4 guide, still rising), ~30× is

not absurd for 40%+ growth and 50%+ margins — if you believe the shortage holds. The

risk is that ~30× forward capitalizes peak-cycle margins as if they're permanent; HDD pricing

is the most cyclical thing in storage. My fair-value read: I'd anchor near the analyst average

(~$547) and pay a discount for cycle risk — fair ~$560–580, not $653. The stock has run ~16%

past even a generous fair read. Position vs range: as extended as it gets — at the high,

maximum distance above its moving averages.

Post-deal / repriced check

No single "deal," but the analog holds: the repricing has already happened. The entry edge

(cheap HDD play before the market priced the AI-storage shortage) was consumed across 2026 as

the stock tripled. Per Wait-for-deal thesis, the old bull case ("WDC is a cheap shortage

beneficiary") is no longer an entry case — it's the consensus, and the price reflects it.

The relevant question is now "is the post-repricing multiple worth owning?" and at $653,

above PTs and into a binary macro event, the answer here is no. The follow-on entry, if any,

comes from a normal dip with the thesis intact — not from paying up at the high.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: A consolidated 3-player industry with frozen supply is selling into uncapped AI

data growth — margins go higher, not lower, and HAMR extends the runway past 2027.

  • Bear: This is a commodity cyclical capitalizing peak margins at ~30× forward, trading

above the targets of the analysts who just upgraded it — the give-back when HDD pricing

normalizes will be violent.

  • First-principles: A $225B HDD maker is defensible only while the shortage lasts; pay for

the cash flows at a cycle-aware multiple, not for the momentum — and not 16% above fair on a

+16% day.

Entry levels the agent derives

  • Buy-below: ~$520 (≈20% below current, near/under analyst avg — a real dip with thesis intact).
  • Fair: ~$560–580 (cycle-discounted view of the post-repricing business).
  • Stretch: ~$620 (top of what I'd justify paying; today's price is above this).
  • Today's $653 is above my stretch level — explicitly not an entry. Entry must be dip-gated,

not at-market. The base rate gives the one counter-argument (below).

Macro fit

All three AI-infra kill-switches clear (capex still being raised, GPU pricing firming, credit

spreads tight) — the demand backdrop behind the HDD shortage is intact. But the macro

stance (2026-06-15) is hold cash through the FOMC: the June 16–17 dot plot (Warsh debut) is

tomorrow and a hawkish "no 2026 cuts / possible hike" median pressures high-multiple names

immediately. WDC at ~30× forward, parabolic and at its high, is exactly the kind of name a

hawkish print de-rates first. On the opportunity-cost lens, the marginal $100 is poorly

spent here: quality name, worst-possible entry geometry, into a binary event. It ranks LOW

on the field right now despite high long-term quality.

Base-rate caveat (must confront): patterns.py extended_run preset (active today, n=19–25)

wins 73% at +21d / 81% at +42d / 79% at +63d, avg +17%/+29%/+44%, max drawdown only ~-6%.

WDC's own history says extended runs in this name tend to keep running. This is a genuine

argument against the HOLD and is why the verdict is HOLD not AVOID — but a strong base rate

doesn't justify buying at-market above analyst PTs the day before the dot plot. The disciplined

expression is a trigger-gated dip entry, which captures most of the continuation edge without

paying the FOMO premium at the high.

Falsification

  • 30-day: FOMC dot plot (06-17) — a dovish surprise that loosens the high-multiple

constraint would raise the score; a hawkish de-rate that pulls WDC toward my $520–580 entry

doesn't decelerate; a guide-down on margin or a HAMR-qualification slip (4 customers) breaks

Research dossier: CRDO — Credo Technology Group Holding

Researched: 2026-06-10 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 35

Business

Credo sells the physical-layer plumbing that moves bits inside AI data centers. Its anchor

product is the AEC (Active Electrical Cable) — copper cables with Credo SerDes silicon

baked into the connector — which has displaced optics for short in-rack/rack-to-rack hops

because it's cheaper and far more reliable at 400G/800G. Around that sit SerDes chiplets,

retimers, line-card DSPs, and now — via the **DustPhotonics acquisition (closed May 27–28,

2026, $750M cash + ~0.92M shares upfront, up to ~4.13M shares total)** — silicon-photonics PIC

capability to bring optical transceivers in-house. Who pays them: hyperscalers building AI

clusters. **Customer concentration is the defining structural fact — the top two customers were

~84% of revenue over the first nine months of FY26, with three hyperscalers each >10% in the

latest quarter.** That is who pays, and also why this revenue is fragile.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business case is real and strengthening; the entry case at $237.59 is not. FY26 (ended

~Apr 2026) revenue was $1.34B, +205% YoY, with Q4 a record $437M (+157% YoY), non-GAAP

gross margin 68.3%, non-GAAP net income $227M in the quarter / $662M for the year. FY27 guidance

is >80% revenue growth, optical alone >$600M, net margin near 50%, FQ1 guide $465–475M.

This is one of the cleanest growth-plus-margin profiles in AI infra. But the catalysts that

would justify paying up have already fired: DustPhotonics closed, and the June 1 beat-and-raise

print is in the tape. The stock is **+103% over 60 trading days, +58.8% vs its 200DMA, +32.8% vs

its 50DMA, and only 9% off its all-time high ($261.38)**. The "is the new thesis worth owning

at the new price" test from extended-with-vs-without-catalyst resolves to: the new thesis is

good, but the new price already reflects it. There is no fresh, un-priced catalyst between now and

the ~late-August FQ1 print.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $237.59 (screen close); market cap $43.84B; shares out 184.45M (fetched).
  • TTM P/E 94.7; P/S ~32.7× on $1.34B trailing revenue — rich on trailing.
  • Forward P/E ~39× (fetched) and, on a FY27 revenue of ~$2.4B (the >80% guide), **forward P/S

~18×** with ~50% net margins. For an 80%-grower throwing off 50% net margin, a high-30s forward

P/E is defensible, not absurd — this is why the verdict is HOLD, not AVOID.

  • Position vs. range: 52-week range $66.75 – $261.38; at $237.59 it sits at ~90% of the high,

+256% off the low. Analyst average 12-mo PT ~$256 (≈8% upside), with a bullish tail

sell-the-news drop on June 1 despite a beat-and-raise** — expectations are sky-high and good news

eased to $237. Mapping to the three post-deal patterns: this is **"sustained repricing" now in its

mature phase** — the multi-week run already happened. Paying up into the catalyst would have

been rational; paying up after it, near the high, is the late-arrival trade.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: An 80%-growth, ~50%-net-margin connectivity monopoly-in-the-making whose optical TAM

just doubled via DustPhotonics — at ~39× forward earnings that's cheap for the growth.

  • Bear: ~84% of revenue rides on two hyperscalers who are actively trying to in-source SerDes;

one capex-digestion quarter or one design loss and a 33× P/S name halves.

Entry levels the agent derives

All three AI-infra kill-switches are clear (capex +77% YoY, GPU lead times still extended, BBB

~100bp) — the secular thesis is intact, so this is a HOLD-for-a-dip, not an AVOID. But the regime

LRCXHOLDfetched35›

Research dossier: LRCX — Lam Research Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-11 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 35

Business

Lam Research is one of the three dominant wafer-fabrication-equipment (WFE) makers, alongside

Applied Materials and Tokyo Electron. Its franchise is etch and deposition — the tools that

carve and layer the 3D structures in leading-edge logic, and especially in 3D NAND where Lam

is the share leader. Customers are the chipmakers themselves (TSMC, Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron,

Intel). This is picks-and-shovels one layer below the picks-and-shovels: Nvidia/hyperscaler

capex pulls HBM and leading-edge logic, which pulls fab buildout, which pulls Lam's etch/dep

tools plus a large recurring service/spares ("CSBG") tail on the installed base. Revenue durably

continues because every node transition and every new fab is more etch/dep-intensive, and the

installed base throws off high-margin service regardless of the equipment cycle.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business thesis is intact and arguably strengthening; the entry thesis is not. FQ3'26

(March qtr) was a record — revenue $5.84B, +24% YoY, adjusted EPS $1.47, +41% YoY, a clean

beat. June-quarter guide is another record: **$6.6B ±$400M, ~50.5% GM, ~36.5% op margin, EPS

$1.65. Management raised the 2026 WFE outlook to $140B (from $135B), with upward bias**, and

flagged advanced-packaging revenue +50% in CY2026 — the direct HBM/AI-leverage line. That is

exactly the kill-switch-#1 signal (capex being raised, not cut), and Lam is a first-order

beneficiary. But the stock has already repriced for all of it: +279% in a year, +60% in 60d, +23%

in 20d, sitting 0.6% off its all-time high and +78% above its 200DMA. You would be paying

the full repriced price at the very top of the range, into next week's FOMC binary. No fresh

(last 7–14 day) catalyst — the print is ~7 weeks old and fully absorbed; the next real catalyst is

the June-quarter report in late July.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $362.52 (Jun 11); 52-week range $87.75–$364.81 → essentially at the high.
  • Market cap $453B; shares 1.25B. TTM revenue $21.68B, +26.5% YoY.
  • Trailing P/E ~61; forward P/E ~40 (sources span 36–43); P/S ~11.5x; PEG ~2.0.
  • For a WFE leader these are top-of-cycle multiples. A justified premium for a thesis-confirmed,

40%+-EPS-growth, high-FCF compounder is ~30–33x forward; that implies fair nearer $300–330 on

the current earnings run-rate. The market is paying ~40x — a momentum/analyst-chase premium

(Cantor PT $425, B. Riley $385) is doing the work above ~$330. The growth can grow into the

multiple over a year, but that is a hold-and-compound case, not an entry-edge case.

Post-deal / repriced check

Not a "deal" name, but the repriced-check applies cleanly via extended-with-vs-without-catalyst:

the Apr-22 print + WFE raise was a genuine thesis-confirming catalyst, and the initial repricing

was rational, not FOMO. But that catalyst is now consumed — price ran a further ~+23% in the 20

days after absorption, on momentum and PT hikes, with nothing new about the business in the last

two weeks. Right now this is "extended without a fresh catalyst" → the wait-don't-chase frame is

the correct one. The entry edge is gone until either a real pullback or the next print.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Etch/dep monopolist riding a WFE cycle that's being raised ($140B) with advanced

packaging +50% — the cleanest direct read-through from AI capex into a profitable, cash-rich name.

  • Bear: ~40x forward and 0.6% off ATH prices perfection; high China exposure + fresh

export-control actions (Hua Hong shipment halt) and post-2025 memory-capex digestion can crack a

top-of-cycle multiple fast (bear-case fair value flagged near $115).

  • First-principles: A $453B WFE leader at ~11.5x sales and 40x forward earnings is a fine

business at a full price — you own the AI buildout's tool-layer, but you're underwriting flawless

execution with no margin of safety at this entry.

Entry levels the agent derives

From this run's data (50DMA ≈ $283, 200DMA ≈ $203; current $362.50):

  • Buy-below: ~$300 — roughly a 15–18% pullback toward the 50DMA / a low-30s forward P/E, where

the risk/reward turns favorable for the $100 book.

  • Fair: ~$330.
  • Stretch: ~$360. At/above the ATH (~$365) you are chasing — do not buy above the high without a

genuinely new catalyst (per principles: no BUY above entry on an already-priced catalyst).

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear, and LRCX's own WFE raise is corroborating evidence for #1 — secular

thesis intact, this name is a beneficiary, not a casualty. But the macro read says **hold cash;

buy only real dips or fresh catalysts; small sizing on high-multiple names into the June 16–17 FOMC**

(first under Warsh, hawkish-surprise risk that pressures premium multiples). On the

Opportunity-cost lens, the marginal $100 at $362 near ATH with no fresh catalyst ranks below

any name still under fair value with a live setup. Its own extended-with-vs-without-catalyst

base rate is unusually supportive (extended_run preset: 75% win +21d, 67% +63d, avg +11.5% at +63d),

so momentum-chasing this specific name has historically paid more often than not — that tempers

the bearishness but does not override paying full price at the top into a binary macro week (worst-case

drawdown in that preset was −39%).

Falsification

  • Flips to BUY: a cohort-wide dip back toward the 50DMA (~$290–300) with kill-switches still

clear; OR a fresh catalyst — the late-July June-qtr print re-rating again, a China export-control

Next earnings: 2026-08-10

Business

Fluence designs, builds, and services utility-scale battery energy storage systems (BESS)

globally — hardware + integration (~85% of revenue) plus a thinner services/software layer

(Mosaic optimization platform, ARR ~$180M FY26 vs $148M FY25). Customers: utilities, renewable

developers, grid operators, and now hyperscalers. Founding institutional shareholders Siemens

and AES. The durable-revenue driver is the AI Energy Thesis flowing into storage: AI data

center load creates power volatility, frequency-regulation, black-start, and load-smoothing

needs that BESS uniquely addresses — Fluence sits alongside behind-the-meter generation rather

than competing with it. The ~$5.6B contracted backlog (10.1 GW, +11% QoQ) is genuine multi-year

revenue visibility by hardware-integrator standards.

This is a commodity-adjacent hardware integrator, not a moat-y software business: adjusted

gross margin ~11% (guided 11–13% FY26), competing against Tesla Megapack, BYD, and Powin. The

revenue multiple is structurally capped by that margin profile — important context for every

valuation claim below.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The current setup at ~$23.71 rests on three legs, two of which have softened on closer

inspection vs. the prior run:

1. Hyperscaler MSAs (May 7, 2026) — master supply agreements with two unnamed major

hyperscalers; Fluence beat ~26 vetted competitors. First binding purchase order "expected

during the quarter" (Q3 FY26, ends June 30). **As of 2026-06-13 no first order has been

announced.** The hard, revenue-bearing catalyst is still pending.

SITMHOLDfetched33›

Research dossier: SITM — SiTime Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.66 · Opportunity score: 33

Business

SiTime makes MEMS-based precision timing semiconductors — oscillators and (post-Renesas) clock ICs that act as the heartbeat in virtually all electronics. MEMS timing is a structural upgrade over quartz: smaller, lower power, superior jitter, no piezoelectric aging. SiTime is the dominant pure-play with ~60% of the MEMS timing market.

Revenue is led by Communications, Enterprise & Datacenter (CED), which grew ~158% YoY in Q1 2026; the rest is mobile, industrial, consumer. AI/datacenter is now the primary engine. The pull-through is real: as AI inference clusters densify (PCIe 5/6, 800G/1.6T Ethernet, high-radix switching), timing tightness rises, so higher GPU density = more MEMS content per rack = a SITM revenue multiplier.

The Renesas timing-business acquisition (announced Feb 4, 2026) adds ~$300M revenue at ~70% gross margin, ~75% from AI/datacenter-comms, and bolts "clock" products onto SITM's "oscillator" line — a full-line timing platform pushing the combined entity toward a $1B revenue run-rate with >60% of revenue AI/datacenter/comms.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business is one of the highest-quality growth stories in semis: Q1 2026 revenue $113.6M (+88% YoY), non-GAAP EPS $1.44 (+23% beat), gross margin 64.5%, operating margin 28% (from 3%), Q2 guided ~$140–150M, full year raised to ≥80% growth. None of that is in dispute.

What changed since the 2026-06-05 run is deal de-risking, not a fresh upside catalyst:

  • HSR antitrust waiting period expired May 8, 2026 — the regulatory tail the prior run flagged is largely gone.
  • Financing is locked: a $1.35B 0% Convertible Senior Notes offering due 2031 closed May 22 (upsized from $1.1B), with a $108M capped call to blunt dilution, replacing most of the $900M Wells Fargo bridge. A 0% coupon is cheap money — the cash side of the $1.5B consideration is funded.

Run through the extended-with-vs-without-catalyst test — what fact about the business changed in the last 7–14 days? Nothing. The convert and HSR clearance are both mid/late-May; over the trailing two weeks the tape is consolidation (ret_20d −11%, off the $901 high to $730), not a fresh repricing. So this is "extended/consolidating without a new catalyst" — the dip is positioning around the debt financing and insider selling, not a thesis upgrade. The de-risk modestly raises my conviction in the business (the deal-funding tail is smaller) without creating an entry edge at $730.

Conclusion: fine to hold, not a buy here. The Q1 beat and the deal are consumed; the stock trades above sell-side consensus into a binary FOMC.

Valuation + position vs. range

Price $729.89; shares ~26.4M → market cap ~$19.3B. 52-week range $186–$902; current = ~81% of high, ~291% above the low (screen: off_52w_high 19%, above_52w_low 291%, ret_60d +112%).

  • TTM revenue $379.9M → ~50.7× trailing sales. Rich even for a category monopoly.
  • Organic FY2026 estimate (Q1 $113.6M + Q2 mid $145M + H2 ~$320M) ≈ $580M → ~33× forward organic sales.
  • Post-close 2027 combined ≈ organic ~$600M + Renesas $300M = ~$900M, on ~30.5M shares (26.4M + 4.13M issued). Add the $1.35B convert (partly offset by deal cash) → EV roughly $20–22B → ~23–25× 2027 combined revenue.
  • The Street has come down to meet the price: consensus 12-mo target clusters $573–608 (median reads $450–574 depending on the panel), i.e. the stock trades above most analyst targets. Roth ($900)/Barclays-type highs are the outliers, not the center.

Priced for continued perfection; the margin of safety is zero.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis, the Renesas acquisition is SITM's deal-equivalent — and the entry edge tied to it is consumed. Classifying the post-deal shape: this is closest to immediate-compression / sustained-repricing already played out — the Feb 4 announcement and the May 6 Q1 beat (the 34% AH spike) were the repricing events; HSR clearance and the 0% convert are confirmations, not new legs. The pre-deal scarcity entry was $186–$300 before AI-timing leverage became consensus. At $730 you are buying the post-deal multiple, and the relevant question is whether ~24× 2027 combined revenue is worth holding — not whether the pre-deal story is real. It is real; it is also fully in the price.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: A MEMS-timing monopoly compounding ~40%+ into the densest AI-rack content opportunity, now with antitrust cleared and the $300M/70%-margin Renesas asset funded at a 0% coupon — clean path to a $1B+ run-rate and $900+.
  • Bear: ~50× trailing sales and above sell-side targets with the FOMC dot-plot 3 days out; any hawkish "no cuts till 2027" print or a single hyperscaler capex wobble reprices this −25–35% to the $450–550 zone, and the new $1.35B convert just added leverage to that downside.
  • First-principles: Precision timing is necessary but lower-criticality silicon than GPUs/HBM; paying ~24× 2027 combined revenue demands AI capex stay red-hot for years — a momentum bet wearing a quality-compounder costume.

The dip-buyer edge is positive but shallow with a fat left tail — consistent with a trigger-gated entry on a deeper pullback, not at-market here.

QCOMHOLDmissing32›

Research dossier: QCOM — Qualcomm Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-09 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 32

Business

Qualcomm designs semiconductor chips and licenses wireless patents across two segments:

  • QCT (chips, ~88% of revenue): Snapdragon SoCs for smartphones, automotive infotainment/ADAS, IoT, and PCs. Revenue Q2 FY2026: $9.5B. Handsets are ~60% of total company revenue; automotive and IoT are the growth legs.
  • QTL (licensing, ~12%): Wireless IP licensing fees (5G/6G patent portfolio), ~$1.3B/quarter, high-margin. This is structurally durable as long as wireless devices are sold.

Revenue durability is mixed: QTL is a near-royalty stream. QCT handsets are tied to Android smartphone shipments (durable but mature) and — critically — Apple (being lost). Automotive and IoT are real and growing but not yet large enough to offset.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The case for buying QCOM today rests on the diversification story: automotive revenue hit a record $1.33B in Q2 (+38% YoY), tracking toward a $6B run rate by FY year-end; the Dragonfly data-center AI brand was launched at Computex (June 1-2); an OpenAI smartphone AI chip partnership was reported on April 27 (+7% on that day). The $20B buyback authorization signals shareholder return confidence. JPMorgan raised its PT to $265 and a June 24 investor day looms as a scheduled catalyst.

Against that: Q3 guidance ($9.2-10.0B revenue, EPS $2.10-2.30 midpoint) is a meaningful step down from Q2's $10.6B. Handsets fell 13% in Q2 and will weaken further. The Apple modem transition is structural — iPhone share ~20% in calendar 2026, ~zero by 2027, implying $5.7-5.9B of annual revenue disappears. Dragonfly at Computex is a brand announcement: AI200 chips launch in 2026, AI250 in 2027 — actual data-center revenue is 12-24 months away. The market sold QCOM ~9% on Computex day, directly contradicting the "repricing on fresh catalyst" frame. Nvidia's RTX Spark AI PC chip adds competitive pressure to Snapdragon X Elite in Windows on Arm.

This is not a BUY now. The near-term fundamentals are weakening, the structural handset headwind has yet to fully land, and the key catalysts (Dragonfly design wins, AI PC ramp) are 12-24 months from maturity. The June 24 investor day could surprise positively — but it's more likely a "sell the news" or neutral event unless concrete data-center design wins are disclosed. The right entry is cheaper.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price (June 8, 2026): $216.81
  • 52-week range: $121.99–$259.92; trading at 83% of 52-week high (~17% below)
  • TTM EPS (non-GAAP, estimated): ~$9.50–10.00 (extrapolating Q2's $2.65 + Q3 midpoint $2.20 + estimated Q1/Q4 at similar cadence)
  • Forward non-GAAP P/E: ~21–23× — not cheap, not extreme for a diversifying semis name
  • Market cap: ~$366B (at $216.81 and ~1.69B diluted shares)
  • Non-handset FY2029 target: $22B (vs. ~$12.5B today) — requires 75% growth in 3 years; automotive tracking well, data center unproven

Fair value frame: At 18-22× normalized forward EPS ($9.50-10.50), fair range is ~$170-230. Current price at $217 sits in the upper half of that band, providing limited upside without execution on Dragonfly or the AI PC ramp. The Apple modem loss — a structural $5-6B revenue headwind peaking in FY2027 — is not fully in the price. When it fully lands, it compresses both revenue and EPS estimates. The stock is neither cheap nor egregiously expensive; it's roughly fair with downside execution risk.

Post-deal / repriced check

QCOM doesn't fit the classic Wait-for-Deal framework (it's a large-cap semiconductor, not a small data-center infra name). However, the relevant question is whether recent positive catalysts (OpenAI partnership, Computex announcements) have consumed the entry edge. The answer is partial:

  • OpenAI smartphone chip: Reported April 27, stock +7%. That repricing is already in.
  • Dragonfly brand launch at Computex: The market sold off ~9% on June 1 despite the announcement. This is the opposite of a positive repricing — the catalyst did not consume upside; it created disappointment. The entry edge from Dragonfly is NOT priced in (market is skeptical); but that also means the catalyst has not validated the bull case.

Conclusion: recent catalysts are largely in the price (OpenAI) or created a sell-the-news dynamic (Dragonfly). The next genuine repricing catalyst is either (a) June 24 investor day with concrete design wins, or (b) Q4 FY2026 earnings showing automotive exceeds $2B/quarter and Dragonfly has traction.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Automotive is a real, growing business at 38% YoY and tracking to $6B run rate — if it reaches $10-15B within 3 years and Dragonfly gets even one major data-center design win, the stock is worth $280-320.
  • Bear: Apple modem transition wipes $5-6B of high-margin handset revenue by FY2027, Q3 guidance is already a step-down, Nvidia now competes directly in AI PCs, and Dragonfly is a brand without a customer — this is a multi-quarter fundamental reset before diversification revenue fills the gap.
  • First-principles: At $366B market cap you're paying ~23× current-year earnings for a business losing its second-largest customer over 18 months while betting on two unproven pivots (data-center chips, AI PCs); that's not a margin-of-safety setup.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived from this run's data only — no anchors carried:

  • Buy-below: $185–195 — reflects the Apple modem impact on FY2027 EPS (could compress to $8.50-9.00 if auto/IoT don't accelerate), discounted 15-18% from current fair value given execution uncertainty
  • Fair: $210–225 — current business value: automotive tracking well, QTL durable, handsets declining but not collapsing immediately; Dragonfly optionality worth modest premium
  • Stretch: $255–270 — only justified if Q4 FY2026 or Q1 FY2027 shows (1) Dragonfly data-center design wins materially pulling forward the $22B non-handset timeline AND (2) automotive exits FY2026 above $7B annualized

The June 24 investor day is the watch event. A miss or vague delivery targets should be bought at $185-195; a genuine design-win announcement should be chased above the current price.

Macro fit

Kill-switch status: None of the three AI-infra kill-switches are tripped (capex growth firm at +67-77% YoY; GPU pricing/lead times normal; credit spreads tight). The secular AI buildout thesis is intact, which underpins QCOM's automotive + edge AI opportunity.

Regime overlay: Market extended (Shiller CAPE 42.3×, forward P/E 21.1×), breadth narrow, CPI at 3.8% pushing rate cuts to fall 2026 or later. This regime argues for: (1) demanding real dips or fresh catalysts before adding, (2) preferring names with near-term revenue visibility over aspirational pivots.

QCOM fails the "fresh catalyst" test at the moment — the Computex event was a sell, not a buy. The Apple modem headwind is timed to peak in FY2027 — which is precisely when the rate environment may still be restrictive. This combination makes now a poor entry.

Opportunity-cost rank vs. the field: QCOM at $217 competes for capital against MRVL (custom AI ASIC momentum, cleaner growth vector), MU (memory AI supercycle with more asymmetric setup), and NVDA itself. In this field, QCOM does not rank as the best marginal use of capital — its near-term thesis is a managed decline (handsets) offset by a multi-year pivot (auto, data center). Opportunity score: 32/100.

  • Flip to AVOID: Q3 guidance misses at the low end; handset revenue collapses faster than expected; investor day guidance is purely aspirational (no design wins named); Nvidia RTX Spark captures 30%+ of AI PC market in first 90 days of sales.

60 days — Q3 FY2026 earnings (expected late July 2026):

  • Flip to BUY: Automotive beats $1.6B+ in Q3 (vs. ~50% YoY guide = ~$1.5B); Dragonfly shows a customer letter of intent; IoT growth accelerates to 15%+ YoY.
  • Confirm HOLD/AVOID: Handsets come in below the low-end of Q3 guidance; Apple modem timeline accelerates to zero faster than FY2027.

90 days — macro:

  • May CPI print (June 11, 2 days away): if hot (3.5%+), rate cut timeline pushes further right, compressing multiple expansion case for QCOM.
  • June 16-17 FOMC: hawkish dots plot would hurt all growth/semis names.

Research dossier: WULF — TeraWulf Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-10 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 31

Business

TeraWulf is a former pure-play bitcoin miner that has pivoted hard into AI/HPC data-center

hosting. It owns power-anchored sites (Lake Mariner NY; the new Abernathy TX campus) and leases

critical IT load to AI compute customers on long-dated contracts. Who pays them: Core42 (G42's

compute arm) and Fluidstack — the latter's lease obligations are credit-backed by Google.

Revenue durability rests on 10–25-year hosting leases with investment-grade credit support, not

on bitcoin price. Q1 2026: $34.0M revenue, of which $21.0M (62%) was HPC lease income — AI

revenue more than doubled vs. Q4 2025 ($9.7M). The thesis is the classic AI Energy Thesis /

permitting-as-binding-constraint play: WULF is a credible-now power+land counterparty while

the grid can't deliver, and labs/neoclouds pay up for that.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business is genuinely transformed and the backlog is large and real: **~$12.8B of contracted

HPC revenue** across Core42 + three Google-backed Fluidstack deals, the newest being a 168 MW

(240 MW gross) Abernathy JV (~$9.5B / 25-yr, WULF 51%, Google backstopping ~$1.3B of Fluidstack's

lease). But "good company" ≠ "good entry." The stock is **~16% off its 52-week high ($27.78),

+41.5% over 60 trading days, +6.5% above its 50-DMA, and +582% off the 52-week low** — fully

repriced for the pivot. The buy case here would require a fresh catalyst or a real dip; today

it has neither. It is up enormously on news that is 6+ months old.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price ~$23.19 (−8.3% on 2026-06-10), market cap ~$11.5B, ~495.5M shares.
  • TTM revenue ~$168M → ~68× trailing sales. TTM net loss −$1.03B (EPS −$2.52) — heavily

non-cash (warrant/derivative remeasurement + the pivot's capex/D&A), but it underscores there

is no GAAP earnings floor here.

  • The multiple only makes sense forward, against the contracted backlog. Even generously: as

Abernathy + existing capacity come online through 2027, run-rate hosting revenue plausibly

reaches ~$1B+/yr — which would put it at ~10× forward contracted run-rate. Reasonable for the

growth IF, and only if, the buildout executes on schedule and on budget ($8–10M/MW capex).

  • The execution gap is the whole story: today's $11.5B cap is paying for revenue that mostly

arrives 2H2026–2028. Buildout slip, cost overrun, or Fluidstack/Google offtake re-cut would

re-rate it down fast. Sits in the top quartile of its range with the cash flows still in the

future.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: WULF is unambiguously post-deal — the entry edge is consumed.

The wait-for-deal frame told you to own this before the Core42/Fluidstack/Google partnerships;

all three landed (the Fluidstack JV Oct 28 2025; financing priced Dec 2025; reaffirmed at Q1

print). Pattern classification: this looks like sustained repricing (BE-post-Oracle shape) —

a multi-quarter run as deal after deal stacked the backlog — now maturing. The follow-on entry,

if any, comes from a normal dip, not from the deals, which are in the price.

Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: the operative question — *what fact about the business

changed in the last 7–14 days?* — has no answer. Nothing fresh. So this is **extended WITHOUT a

fresh catalyst = noise/FOMO; wait, don't chase.** Per warrant-conditionality-equity-announcements:

the Google "backing" is a credit backstop on Fluidstack's lease (real, derisks the offtake) — not

cash into WULF; and WULF funded its side with a dilutive $1.0B raise at $19 in April 2026

(54.5M new shares), so the share count has already absorbed the buildout financing.

Council (one line each)

  • Bull: $12.8B of investment-grade-backed contracted backlog at ~10× forward run-rate — if

Abernathy lands on time, the stock grows into and past today's price (Morgan Stanley $66.50).

  • Bear: 68× trailing sales, −$1B loss, fresh $1B dilution, and the entire valuation rests on

a not-yet-built Texas campus executing flawlessly into an extended, narrow market.

  • First-principles: A power+land lessor with credit-backed 10–25-yr offtake is a real

business — but you're paying a 2028 price today, with no margin of safety and no fresh edge.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived this run (price $23.19; high $27.78; April raise $19; 50-DMA ~$21.8):

  • Buy-below: ~$17 — a genuine flush back toward/under the April raise base, where execution

risk is better compensated. Base rates demand patience here (see Macro fit).

  • Fair: ~$22–24 — roughly where it trades; fairly valued for the backlog with execution risk.
  • Stretch: ~$27–28 — at/near the 52-wk high; only justified on a new committed-capacity

deal or de-risking milestone (Abernathy energization), not at-market.

Do not buy at-market: it's above where the payoff is asymmetric and there's no fresh catalyst.

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear (ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches): secular demand intact, so single-name

weakness is noise/opportunity, not thesis break. But the regime (2026-06-10 macro read) is

extended, narrow, multiple-driven, with the Fed sidelined by a hot 4.2% headline CPI — the

overlay explicitly says demand a real dip OR a fresh catalyst, hold cash by default. WULF offers

neither right now. Entry-shape base rates (patterns.py): the active extended_run preset wins

62% at +63d (avg +31.7%) — but with avg −21.9% / worst −85.4% drawdown; and the dip_buyer

preset is weak (48.6% win, avg −2.4% at +63d). Translation: chasing the extended run has worked

historically but is violently volatile, and shallow dips don't pay — so any entry must be

trigger-gated on a real flush, never at-market. On the Opportunity-cost lens, the marginal

$100 has better homes than an extended post-deal name with no fresh catalyst.

Falsification

  • 30d: A fresh committed-cash capacity deal (new lab/neocloud offtake, not envelope language)

or Abernathy construction de-risking → flips toward BUY via extended-with-vs-without-catalyst.

  • 60d: Q2 print (late July/Aug) showing HPC revenue ramp on plan and capex on budget; vs. any

slip/overrun or further dilution → confirms HOLD or pushes toward AVOID.

  • 90d: A real cohort-wide dip (macro-cohort-confirmation) carrying WULF toward ~$17 with the

backlog intact → flips to BUY. A Fluidstack/Google offtake renegotiation or financing wobble →

AVOID.

Research dossier: AMAT — Applied Materials

Researched: 2026-06-10 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 30

Business

Applied Materials is the largest wafer-fab-equipment (WFE) maker — the deepest picks-and-shovels

layer of the whole AI stack. It sells deposition, etch, ion-implant, CMP and metrology tools (plus

a large, sticky services arm, AGS) to every leading-edge foundry, logic and memory fab on earth

(TSMC, Samsung, Intel, SK hynix, Micron). Revenue durably continues because *no one builds a chip

without this gear*: it's a 3-4 player oligopoly (AMAT/LRCX/KLA + ASML in litho), tools are

process-qualified for years, and AGS recurs off the installed base. This is structurally a much

better moat than the wait-for-deal small-caps in the watchlist — it doesn't depend on a single

lab signing a single contract. The cyclicality is the catch: WFE spend is downstream of customer

capex cycles and exposed to China export controls.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business case is real and improving; the entry case is not. Q2 2026 (reported May 15) was

a record: revenue $7.91B (+11% YoY), non-GAAP GM 50.0% (highest in 25+ years), op margin 32.1%,

EPS $2.86 (+20% YoY). Q3 guide $8.95B ±$500M / EPS $3.36 — implying re-acceleration. Management

raised semicap-equipment growth to >30% for 2026 and advanced packaging to >50%, both

AI-driven (leading-edge logic, DRAM/HBM, advanced packaging). That's a genuine thesis-confirming

catalyst — so this is "extended with a catalyst," not pure FOMO (extended-with-vs-without-catalyst).

But the operative question that frame demands is "is the new thesis worth owning at the new price?"

— and at $497 the answer is no. The repricing has already happened.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $497.06; market cap ~$395B; shares ~794M. Off 52w high only 7%; +62.8% vs 200DMA,

+43.5% in 60 days, +15.3% in 20 days — about as extended as the screen gets.

  • Trailing P/E ~47x; forward P/E ~34x (implied NTM EPS ~$14.7). TTM revenue $29.0B but only

+3.3% YoY; FY25 earnings actually fell 2.5% to $7.0B. So the run is almost entirely

multiple expansion — AMAT has re-rated from its historical ~15-20x cyclical-semicap band to

a ~34x forward AI-infra multiple on the strength of accelerating recent prints, not on a step-

change in trailing fundamentals yet.

  • My fair-value read: a high-quality oligopoly WFE name in an AI upcycle deserves a premium to its

cyclical history, but 34x forward on mid-teens forward growth (PEG ~2+) is full. At ~28x × ~$14.7

NTM EPS → ~$410 fair; 24x (haircut for WFE cyclicality + China risk) → ~$352; 32x (AI premium

sustained) → ~$470. Current $497 sits above even the generous end of that band. Sell-side

agrees the upside is thin: consensus PT ~$511 (~3% above spot), bull case Mizuho $540 (+8.6%),

street low $358.

Post-deal / repriced check

AMAT is not a wait-for-deal small-cap, but the Wait-for-deal thesis post-deal logic maps

cleanly: the **AI re-rating catalyst (earnings beat + raised >30% guide + Broadcom/SCREEN

partnerships + PT hikes) has been consumed. Shape = sustained repricing** (BE-post-Oracle

pattern), not sell-the-news — the stock ran for weeks on follow-through (June 8 +8.6% on partnership

news). The edge of paying up under that frame exists only while the re-rate has room; here the

multiple has expanded to ~34x forward and consensus PT is ~3% away. The old bull case is no longer

an entry case.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Deepest, most durable moat in the AI stack — every fab needs these tools — and semicap

is re-accelerating (>30% 2026 guide) with record 50% margins; own the shovels, not the miners.

  • Bear: You're paying 47x trailing / 34x forward for a name that grew revenue 3.3% TTM, is 7%

off its high and +63% vs its 200DMA, with WFE cyclicality and China-export-control tail risk —

a multiple round-trip to even ~28x is a ~$410 stock (-17%).

  • First-principles: Great business, wrong price — at a ~$395B cap and a 34x forward multiple,

the marginal $100 buys ~3% to consensus and eats the full de-rating risk if the AI capex tape

wobbles.

Entry levels the agent derives

  • Buy-below: ~$420 (≈29x forward; requires a real, cohort-confirmed dip per macro-cohort-confirmation).
  • Fair: ~$455.
  • **Stretch (only on a fresh catalyst, e.g. another guide raise): ~$480.**
  • Spot $497 is above even my stretch → no buy at market. Entry must be trigger-gated, not at-market

— consistent with the base rate (see Macro fit).

Macro fit

  • Kill-switches (all clear): capex FAR from tripping (~$725B 2026, +77%) — directly supports

AMAT's WFE demand; GPU pricing/lead times tight; credit spreads tight. Secular thesis intact, so

single-name weakness would be opportunity — but there's no weakness here to buy.

  • Regime: market extended, breadth narrow, S&P fwd P/E ~21x, gains multiple-driven; no rate cut

expected at June 16-17 FOMC, first cut not until Q4 2026 at the earliest. That macro explicitly

says multiple expansion on high-P/E names is not earned — and AMAT at 34x forward, 7% off its

high, is exactly the kind of name a leadership-cohort de-rate would hit hardest.

  • Entry-shape base rate (AMAT, extended_run preset, n≈21): +63d win 65%, avg +5.9%, but worst

drawdown -35%. Momentum-favorable but fat left tail — confirms a trigger-gated entry over chasing.

  • Opportunity-cost rank: mid-low. Best-in-class business, but at this price the marginal $100

has ~3% to consensus and carries a full de-rate downside. Loses to any name still below fair value

with a live setup. Hold cash / wait for the dip.

Falsification

  • Flips to BUY (30/60/90d): (30d) a cohort-wide AI-infra de-rate drags AMAT to ~$420 on macro,

not name-specific bad news; (60d) another guidance raise (>35% 2026 semicap, China normalization,

or an HBM/advanced-packaging step-up) that re-accelerates growth enough to justify the multiple;

(90d) rate-cut path reopens (energy rolls over, Fed signals a fall cut), easing the multiple

constraint on high-P/E names.

  • Flips to AVOID: hyperscaler Q2 prints (late July) show capex deceleration (kill-switch #1) —

AMAT is highly levered to that; a China export-control shock; or DRAM/HBM pricing rollover that

pulls forward the next WFE down-cycle.

Research dossier: CBRS — Cerebras Systems Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 30

Next earnings: 2026-06-23

Business

Cerebras builds wafer-scale AI silicon — a single dinner-plate-sized chip (~58× the area

of a GPU die), sold as integrated CS-3 systems and as cloud inference capacity. The pitch is

fast inference: company-claimed ~15× GPU speed on open-source models. Who pays them: today,

overwhelmingly UAE state-linked entities (MBZUAI ~62%, G42 ~24% of FY2025 revenue — 86%

combined); forward, OpenAI (a $20B+, 750MW contract through 2028, the bulk of a $24.6B RPO

backlog) and, newly, AWS (agreed to offer WSE to cloud customers; Amazon reportedly bought

~$270M of stock). This is the only genuine NVDA-competitor silicon bet in the universe —

structurally different from the GPU-cloud resellers (CRWV/NBIS) and miners-to-AI (IREN).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

No — fine company, wrong price/time. The day-1 IPO pop the user flagged has unwound exactly

as predicted: $386 high → $214, a 44% give-back, now basing near its all-time low ($196.73).

But the give-back didn't make it cheap — at ~92× TTM P/S ($47B cap / $510M rev) CBRS is still

the single most expensive name in the field, on a business with negative non-GAAP operating

income (-$75.7M FY25), -$623M TTM FCF, and 86% UAE customer concentration. Two binary events sit

inside the next 10 days: the FOMC dot plot (June 16–17) — the macro read's named swing event,

which most directly threatens extreme-P/S names — and the first-ever public print (June 23),

a high-variance event with gross-margin (OpenAI price-protection terms) and concentration scrutiny

risk. Buying the marginal $100 at $214 into a double-binary, with no fresh thesis-confirming

catalyst that isn't already priced, is paying full freight before the coin flips.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $214 (2026-06-12 close); 52w range $196.73–$386.34; ~44% off high, ~9% above low.
  • ~92× TTM P/S, P/E 154 (GAAP, distorted by a one-time $363M G42 paper gain). No clean

fundamentals anchor exists — every multiple is circular because it floats on the highest P/S

ever documented for a chip company at this revenue scale.

  • Backlog $24.6B (~48× FY25 rev) gives unusual visibility if it converts; sell-side models

$510M FY25 → ~$5.5B FY28. That ramp is the entire bull case and is unproven at scale.

  • My fair-value read: there's no defensible DCF here; I anchor off offering mechanics and the

fade. Roughly fair ~$200–215 (≈where it trades — strong backlog/initiations offset by extreme

multiple); a real entry needs a margin of safety below that, toward the $185 IPO floor.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: CBRS is post-deal and post-IPO — the OpenAI deal (Jan 2026) and

AWS term sheet are disclosed and baked in; the entry-side scarcity edge is consumed. Of the three

post-deal shapes, this is tracking sell-the-news fade: gapped to $386 on day-1 sentiment, bled

to $214 as stabilization expired, the quiet period ended, and retail flippers exited. The fade is

mostly name-specific IPO mechanics, not a cohort dip — so macro-cohort-confirmation says

this is price discovery settling, not a confirmed macro dip to buy. The strong June-7 analyst

initiations (avg ~$294 PT, all Buy/OW) are the one genuinely new datapoint vs. the user's dive —

but they popped the stock +17% on June 10 and it has since faded back below the pop. Initiations

are priced and bleeding, not a fresh reason to chase. Note: AWS's ~$270M share purchase is a

committed-cash piece (unlike a bare out-of-money warrant per

warrant-conditionality-equity-announcements) — a real, if small, positive signal.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Only credible commercial alt-NVDA silicon, OpenAI co-design lock-in + AWS distribution + $24.6B backlog; if the ramp converts, 92× P/S compresses to <15× on FY28 and today looks cheap.
  • Bear: 92× P/S, 86% UAE-state revenue, US revenue down 34% YoY, negative operating cash flow, a Nov lockup of ~155M insider shares against a ~220M float — a high-multiple sell-the-news with the supply cliff still ahead.
  • First-principles: A wafer-scale moonshot proven for one sovereign cohort over five years, now betting it wins 10–15% of inference from NVDA; that EV is worth ~$25–40B today, not $47B — the price still leads the proof.

Entry levels the agent derives

Re-derived this run, not inherited:

  • Buy-below ≤ $185 — IPO offer floor; ~14% below spot; the level where day-1 buyers capitulate and the multiple compresses toward the range sovereign customers underwrote. Pull only with event/cohort confirmation (ideally after the FOMC + first print clear).
  • Fair ~$200–215 — roughly current; not a buy zone, just where backlog/initiations balance the extreme multiple.
  • Stretch / don't-chase > $260 — initiation-pop territory; new money here pays post-execution pricing pre-execution.

At $214 we sit between buy-below and fair — close to fair, not at a discount. No edge for the marginal dollar.

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear (ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches) — secular thesis intact. But the

discount-rate sizing constraint bites hardest here: the 2026-06-13 macro read names the **June

16–17 dot plot** as the proximate catalyst that could de-rate high-P/S leaders, and CBRS at ~92×

P/S is the most extreme-P/S name in the universe — precisely the cohort a hawkish "no cuts until

2027" dot plot pressures first. Stance is *hold cash into the FOMC, small size on high-P/S names,

buy only real dips or fresh catalysts.* On the Opportunity-cost lens, the marginal $100 ranks

low: CRWV/NBIS/IREN carry stronger near-term thesis confirmation, and cash is the macro-blessed

default into a double-binary week. CBRS earns a watch slot, not a buy.

Falsification

  • 30-day (the binaries): Hawkish dot plot + a soft June-23 first print → likely flushes toward

the $185 floor (or below) on margin/concentration scrutiny — that's the entry, not a thesis break.

A clean print (OpenAI ramp on schedule, margins holding) + dovish-enough FOMC → sustained

repricing, verdict moves toward BUY on confirmation even at a higher price.

  • 60-day: AWS contract converts to committed offtake / a second non-UAE anchor lands → concentration

kill-switch eases, re-rate justified.

  • 90-day: Nov ~180-day lockup (~155M shares) digestion — heavy supply into paper-gain holders would

reopen a deep sub-$150 entry; orderly absorption removes the last structural overhang.

Research dossier: CIFR — Cipher Digital Inc. (Cipher Mining)

Researched: 2026-06-10 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 30

Business

Cipher is a Bitcoin miner converting itself into a hyperscale data-center landlord ("power-shell

lessor"). It owns power capacity, interconnect rights, and shells in Texas/Ohio and leases that capacity

to hyperscalers on long-dated contracts rather than operating the GPUs itself. Today's cash flow is still

the Odessa BTC mine (207 MW, ~11.6 EH/s, ~346 BTC mined in Q1, sub-$3/kWh power) — a declining bridge.

The investable story is the HPC pivot: 907 MW operating + contracted (700 MW contracted gross HPC),

three campus leases with "leading hyperscalers" (one signed Q1 2026 with an "investment-grade"

tenant; the named AWS 300 MW + Fluidstack 168 MW from earlier sit underneath), and a roadmap to **~4.2 GW

by 2030**. Management calls 2026 "the year of execution." Who pays them: hyperscalers paying contracted

rent on energized MW — but first meaningful HPC revenue doesn't start until late 2026 (Barber Lake).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Largely no, not at ~$21. The contract book is real and the counterparty quality is the best in the

mining-to-AI cohort, but every dollar of HPC revenue is still in the future, the multiple has expanded

since April, and the entry edge is gone. The bull case (an $11.4B backlog re-rating the equity) is

intact as a thesis — it is not an entry case at a 41x trailing-sales price with zero HPC revenue on

the board and two straight earnings misses behind it. This is a "watch for a real dip" name, not a buy.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price ~$21.02 (market cap $8.60B, 409M shares). 52-week range $3.29–$28.62 — trades ~27%

off the high (screen: 26.5% off high, +26% above 200DMA, +38.9% over 60d → extended within a dip).

  • TTM revenue $209.8M → P/S ≈ 41x (was 34.7x in the user's April dive — the multiple got richer,

not cheaper, even though the print missed). Deeply unprofitable: FY25 net loss ~-$822M, TTM net

-$898M / EPS -$2.29 (dominated by non-cash impairments + convertible-note mark-to-market, but OCF was

also negative in April's read).

  • Forward frame: analyst 2027 revenue consensus ~$835M → ~10.3x 2027 sales on today's cap, before

debt. With the new Stingray notes the EV multiple is higher. For a power-shell lessor whose steady-state

economics are mid-teens-IRR triple-net leases (not compute-operator margins), ~10x future sales is a

full price that bakes in on-schedule energization of 700 MW. My fair value: ~$15–20 today; $21 is

the top of that band, $24+ prices in 2028 optionality not yet de-risked.

  • Today's tape: fell -8.75% on the session (intraday $23.59 high → $21.11 low) — a visible

give-back, plausibly the $810M-note leverage/Bitfury-selling reaction. Worth watching as the start of a

post-run cool-off rather than a bottom.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: CIFR is fully post-deal and has continued to reprice UP. The

classification is "sustained repricing" — the stock ran through a soft Q1 print to a new 52-week

high ($28.62) on the contracted-revenue narrative + sell-side initiations (Bernstein Outperform / Jefferies

Buy, both $32), then started giving some back. The pre-deal scarcity edge is consumed; the only

execution financing (a positive on the non-dilutive funding path, but not a new contract), and analyst

initiations are not catalysts. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, extended + no fresh catalyst →

don't chase.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: An $11.4B contracted backlog with investment-grade hyperscalers, funded non-dilutively

($2B bond + $810M Stingray notes), with the Street initiating at $32 — the equity re-rates as 700 MW

energizes through 2026–27.

  • Bear: You're paying 41x trailing sales and ~10x 2027 sales for a power-shell REIT that has missed

two prints in a row, has negative and shrinking current revenue, stacking debt, with insiders

  • First-principles: $8.6B for $210M of negative-margin TTM revenue only makes sense if the contracted

not when — and "when" is the entire valuation gap.

Research dossier: MARA — MARA Holdings, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-16 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 30

Next earnings: 2026-07-28

Business

A bitcoin miner reinventing itself as an "energy and digital infrastructure" company. The cash engine today is still BTC block rewards (~$174.6M Q1 revenue at ~45% gross margin), denominated in a volatile asset they mark to market — so the GAAP P&L swings with bitcoin, not operations (Q1 net loss −$1.26B, ~$1.0B of it non-cash BTC mark). The forward story is converting ~1.9 GW of energized power + land into contracted AI/HPC tenancy: the Starwood JV (Feb 2026, ~1 GW near-term → 2.5 GW path) and the $1.5B Long Ridge acquisition (505 MW gas plant + 1,600 acres in PJM/Ohio, >1 GW path). Revenue durably continues only if those megawatts get signed hyperscaler tenants — today they don't.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

No — it's a WATCH/HOLD, not an entry. The entire bull case is a multiple swap: MARA trades at ~6.3× P/S (miner math) vs cohort peers priced as AI-infra (CIFR ~35×, CORZ ~24×, IREN ~21×). The instant a hyperscaler signs on Starwood/Long Ridge, the market is forced to re-rate the energized GW — a 6.3×→15× move ~doubles the equity at flat revenue. But that catalyst still has not fired as of 2026-06-16: it remains "advanced conversations with multiple prospective tenants," same language as the Q1 call. The only news since the user's 2026-05-31 dive is the June 11 clearance of bondholder consents on Long Ridge's 8.75% 2032 notes (avoids a change-of-control redemption at 101% — removes a financing landmine). That is a real de-risking of the acquisition, but it is not a tenant signing and not a repricing event; the entry thesis is unchanged. First AI/critical-IT capacity isn't targeted live until ~mid-2028 — the optionality is years out, not quarters.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $14.42, market cap $5.50B, 381.3M shares. 52-week range $6.66–$23.45: 38.5% off the high, +116% off the low.
  • P/S ~6.3× on TTM revenue $867.8M. Note the temporal trap: stockanalysis shows TTM revenue +23.1% YoY, but that includes strong trailing quarters — the recent trend is the opposite, declining sequentially ($252M→$238M→$202M→$174.6M, −18% YoY in Q1). The accelerating-ramp story the multiple needs is not in the numbers yet.

The P/S bands bracket "6.3× ± dispersion," they do not validate 6.3× as right — the cheap multiple is simultaneously the bull thesis (too low for an AI-infra asset) and the bear thesis (correct for melting BTC mining). The market is paying full price for proven mining + near-full credit for unproven conversion. Little margin of safety at spot.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: MARA is genuinely the cohort's pre-deal name — it owns the scarce input (energized GW + premium PJM siting) but is the least contracted of the major mining-to-AI names (IREN signed NVIDIA+Microsoft, CORZ signed CoreWeave, CIFR signed AWS+Fluidstack; MARA has signed none). So the entry edge is NOT consumed — but it is also unrealized and date-less. The bondholder-consent clearance does not convert it. This is the rare case where the wait-for-deal trigger is still live precisely because the deal hasn't landed.

NVMIHOLDfetched30›

Research dossier: NVMI — Nova Ltd

Researched: 2026-06-12 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.65 · Opportunity score: 30

Next earnings: 2026-08-13

Business

Nova Ltd is an Israeli semiconductor process-control / metrology vendor — picks-and-shovels

at the chip-manufacturing substrate. It sells optical, X-ray, and materials metrology tools (plus

the software/modeling that interprets them) that fabs use to measure dimensions, films, and

composition during production. Customers are the leading-edge foundries/IDMs and memory makers

(TSMC, Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron, Intel-class). Revenue durably continues because metrology

intensity rises every node: more layers, EUV/advanced patterning, gate-all-around, and — the

current driver — DRAM/HBM and 2.5D/3D advanced packaging for AI accelerators all need more

measurement steps per wafer. This is a genuine AI-infra beneficiary one layer below the GPU.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business case is strong; the entry case is weak. Q1 2026 (reported 2026-05-14) was a

record: revenue $235.3M (+6% QoQ, +10% YoY, above guidance), non-GAAP EPS $2.33, gross

margin 57.7%, operating margin ~34%. Memory was the standout at 34% of product revenue

(record), advanced packaging also a record — i.e. the AI-driven mix (HBM/DRAM + packaging) is

exactly what's carrying it. Q2 guide non-GAAP EPS $2.34–$2.48 = continued strength. But the

stock is at/near its 52-week high (~$583 vs ~$587 high, 0.8% off), **+141% over the past

year, +27% over 60 days, +45% above its 200DMA, and just ran +10% on June 11** with no

fresh company news that day (looks like semicap-cohort momentum + a cluster of sell-side PT raises,

not a new fact). Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst the operative test is: *what changed

about the business in the last 7–14 days?* The print was a month ago; nothing thesis-confirming has

landed since. That makes the recent run extended-without-catalyst → don't chase.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price ~$583, market cap ~$18.5B, ~31.8M shares.
  • Revenue TTM $902.5M (+21% TTM; FY2025 $880.6M, +31% YoY). Net income FY2025 $259M

(~29% net margin), EPS ~$7.97.

  • P/E TTM ~73x · forward P/E ~52x · P/S ~20.5x. vs the name's own 5-yr median P/E ~36x — so

the multiple has roughly doubled off the AI re-rate. GF Value (DCF-style) pegs ~$305, calling

it ~84% overvalued; I think that's too harsh (it penalizes the durable re-rate), but it frames

how much optimism is in the tape.

  • My fair-value read: best-in-class process control deserves a premium, but ~52x forward / 20x

sales is priced for sustained 20%+ growth and a held multiple. Annualizing the Q2 guide (~$9.5–

10 FY-run-rate EPS) at a defensible ~38–42x forward = ~$380–430 fair. Current price sits well

above my fair band — most of the next year of execution is already in it.

  • Entry-shape base rates (patterns.py, 1273 bars): the active extended_run preset (28

instances, matches today) → +63d win 66.7%, avg +8.6%, but avg drawdown −12.3% /

worst −33.9%. The dip_buyer preset does better on payoff: +63d win 65.7%, avg +12.8%.

Translation: this name's own history says buying the dip beats chasing the extension — the

entry should be trigger-gated to a pullback, not at-market at the high.

Post-deal / repriced check

Wait-for-deal thesis doesn't apply cleanly — NVMI is not a pre-deal small-cap infra play

waiting for a lab partnership; it's an established, profitable equipment vendor. But the spirit

of the discipline does apply: the AI-metrology re-rate (memory/HBM + packaging) is **already in the

multiple** (P/E ~36x → ~73x). The old "AI lifts metrology intensity" bull case is now the

consensus, not an edge. Buying here is paying the repriced multiple at the high — the entry edge

has been consumed even though the business edge is intact.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Indispensable, rising-intensity toll-taker on every leading-edge wafer, with HBM/DRAM

and advanced-packaging tailwinds compounding through the AI buildout — quality you hold for years.

  • Bear: ~73x trailing / ~20x sales at an all-time high, into an FOMC dot-plot that specifically

  • First-principles: Wonderful business, wrong price — at this multiple the next good quarter

Do not buy at the current ~$583 absent such a catalyst — that's the exact extended-no-catalyst

Macro fit

All three ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches are clear (capex +77% YoY, GPU lead times 6–12mo, HY

RMBSHOLDfetched30›

Research dossier: RMBS — Rambus Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 30

Next earnings: 2026-07-27

Business

Rambus sells memory interface chips — the DDR5 server DIMM chipset (Registering Clock

Drivers / RCDs, companion/data-buffer chips, temperature sensors, power-management) that sits

between the CPU and the DRAM modules — plus silicon IP (security IP, interface IP licensed

into SoCs) and a legacy patent-licensing stream. Customers are the DIMM module makers and

hyperscaler/server OEMs; they pay because every DDR5 server module needs an RCD, and Rambus

holds ~mid-40% RCD share. The durable revenue is the per-module silicon content: as the

industry steps DDR5 → MRDIMM, silicon content per module rises, expanding the dollar TAM even

at flat unit volumes. The AI/data-center memory-bandwidth build is the secular tailwind.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Healthy, profitable, growing — but not a buy at $146. Q1 FY2026 (reported 2026-04-27) was a

solid, in-line print, not a blowout: GAAP revenue $180.2M, product revenue $88.0M (+15% YoY,

DDR5-driven), licensing billings $70.8M — a "reality check as expectations reset" quarter. The

stock is nonetheless up +57% in 60 days and +12% in 20 days, sitting 15.8% off its

52-week high and +38% above its 200-DMA. That move is cohort momentum — the 2026 memory /

DRAM-tightness re-rating that lifted the whole memory-and-semicap complex (Micron, the

advanced-packaging semicap upcycle in resolved-themes.md) — **not a fresh RMBS-specific

catalyst.** No June deal, no June product surprise; the MRDIMM ramp is a known late-2026/2027

forward story, already in the narrative. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: the

business-fact test ("what changed in the last 7–14 days?") comes back empty for the name itself,

so this is extended without a fresh name-specific catalyst → don't chase.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price ~$146.5, market cap ~$15.85B, ~108M shares.
  • Trailing P/E ~70×; forward P/E ~46× on 2026E EPS ~$2.94 (+20%). 2026E revenue ~$818M

(+15.6%); TTM revenue $721M (+19%); net income ~$230M.

  • That's a premium multiple for ~mid-teens-to-20% growth (fwd PEG ~2.3–2.9). Defensible for a

high-margin IP + sticky-content franchise, but it leaves no margin of safety at $146.

  • Position vs. range: near the top of its 52-week range (15.8% off high, +153% above low),

+12% vs 50-DMA (~$131), +38% vs 200-DMA (~$106). Extended.

  • Sell-side fair value sits AT the price: consensus PT ~$145 (stockanalysis, 9 analysts) /

$135.67 (14 analysts, Jun 2) — i.e. ~0% to −8% to consensus. Range $100–$180 (Evercore $172

bull, Baird $120, low $100). The average analyst sees the stock as already at fair value.

Post-deal / repriced check

RMBS is not a Wait-for-deal thesis pre-deal small-cap — it's an established profitable

franchise. The relevant version of the check: has the move already consumed the edge? Yes,

on the entry side. The 60-day re-rating priced in the memory-cycle optimism and the MRDIMM

content story ahead of the volume ramp; consensus PT is now level with the price. The secular

thesis (more silicon content per module, AI memory demand) is intact and ownable — but at $146

the entry edge has been repriced away. Owning it here is paying full freight for a forward

ramp that doesn't show up in revenue until late 2026/2027.

SNOWHOLDfetched30›

Research dossier: SNOW — Snowflake

Researched: 2026-06-16 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.7 · Opportunity score: 30

Next earnings: 2026-08-26

Business

Snowflake is the "AI Data Cloud" — a cloud-native data warehouse / lakehouse sold on consumption:

customers buy compute credits as they query and move data, so revenue tracks how much enterprise

data work actually runs on the platform. Who pays: ~10K enterprises across financial services,

healthcare, retail, tech; 779 accounts each spending >$1M TTM (+29% YoY) as of Q1 FY2027. Why it

durably continues: once data is structured and governed inside Snowflake it doesn't move easily;

126% NRR confirms customers expand faster than they churn; cross-cloud support (AWS/Azure/GCP) is

a moat the hyperscalers' own data services can't replicate for multi-cloud shops.

AI pivot (the live driver): CEO Sridhar Ramaswamy is rotating data warehouse → data platform →

agentic AI runtime. The Q1 acceleration was attributed specifically to Cortex Code ("CoCo"),

Snowflake's AI coding agent (GA Feb 5, 2026), which the company called the strongest sequential

dollar growth in its history. Snowflake Cortex (in-governance LLM inference), Snowflake

Intelligence, and the May 2026 Natoma acquisition (enterprise MCP governance for AI agents) are the

agentic stack. The thesis: every enterprise AI agent needs governed, trusted data access and

Snowflake owns that layer. This is an orchestration-flavored revenue line riding on its data

substrate — genuinely interesting, but already the reason the May print beat.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

No — quality name, wrong entry. This run reaches the same conclusion as the 2026-06-08 dossier,

and the tape has handed me a clean confirmation: eight days later the stock is flat (~$238 then,

$238.32 now), having held the post-earnings gap without extending or giving back. That tells me the

36% repricing was real and is now the resting price — not a setup that's still resolving in my

favor.

What changed since May 27: nothing thesis-altering. The catalyst stack (Q1 beat + $6B AWS deal +

Summit + Natoma + guidance raise) all landed May 27–June 2. Recent green days (+4.5%) were macro —

falling Treasury yields and Summit afterglow — not a fresh, not-yet-priced Snowflake fact. Per

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst the operational test is "what fact about the business changed

in the last 7–14 days?" Answer: none. So the FOMO frame is the correct frame here, and the next hard

data point (Q2 FY2027) is Aug 26 — ~10 weeks out.

Valuation + position vs. range

Current price: ~$238.32 (Jun 16, 2026) · Market cap: $82.6B · Shares: 346.6M
